#2280. Kim Hellberg must make Middlesbrough change after Wrexham defeat – ‘monumental’ defender could drop to the bench: Opinion

Middlesbrough saw their 11-game unbeaten run come to an end in a 2-0 defeat to Wrexham on Wednesday night. It was a disappointing result for Boro, who lacked their usual attacking threat with just two shots on target registered from 62.1% possession (BBC Sport).

Thankfully, Kim Hellberg has a good opportunity to bounce back to winning ways here, but he must consider changes. The Swede’s approach of ‘relationism’ encourages player freedom, so there aren’t positions as such, but he started with three centre-backs against the Red Dragons as Luke Ayling was reintroduced.

The decision didn’t give Boro any extra defensive security, and they missed an extra body in attack. It means that, with Watford up next on Saturday, Hellberg must switch to his previous setup.

As said before, Hellberg doesn’t select his team with formations in mind. But, the personnel and roles his team plays usually matches up to a 4-2-3-1, so he sacrificed a body in attack against Wrexham to bring Ayling in.

The 35-year-old is a key influence for Hellberg, with his ability to push up the right-hand side making him a good fit for the right centre-back role in his fluid setup. Speaking upon the announcement of Ayling’s new deal earlier this year, the Middlesbrough boss labelled him as “monumental”.

However, if a centre-back is to drop out of the side against Watford, it seems likely to be him. Adilson Malanda and Ashley Phillips are both key assets, and have been Hellberg’s preferred centre-backs when he picks two for his starting XI.

Dropping Ayling would facilitate a start for another more forward-thinking player. Of those who were on the bench against Wrexham, Australian midfielder Riley McGree could have the energy and creative spark that Boro were missing on Wednesday.

Other options for Hellberg to pick from include Myles Peart-Harris and Jeremy Sarmiento.

Hellberg nearly always plays with one striker, so that is unlikely to change against Watford. However, with star man Will Lankshear still a doubt for Middlesbrough, there may be a switch in personnel.

Kyle Joseph came into the starting XI in North Wales, with Tommy Conway tending to operate more on the left-hand side. The latter could move up top, having spent much of his career as a centre forward.

Alternatively, Hellberg could hand a first league start of the season to David Strelec. The Slovakian striker has made two appearances off the bench in the past week as he rebuilds his fitness following an injury-hit campaign.

Hellberg will be sure to weigh up his options carefully as he bids to get Boro back to winning ways before the international break.

#2279. Frenkie De Jong hits back at critics and sends Hansi Flick message

By Naim Beneddra | 04 Nov 2026 19:44

Barcelona midfielder Frenkie de Jong has expressed his frustration at the constant speculation surrounding him, while making it clear that he wants to reclaim a regular starting place under Hansi Flick.

The Netherlands international spoke out after reaching 300 appearances for the Catalan giants during Tuesday's 3-0 Champions League victory over Aston Villa, having recently returned from a lengthy injury absence.

De Jong believes that his performances, fitness and future attract considerably more scrutiny than those of his teammates, particularly when he is unavailable for selection.

However, the 29-year-old also delivered an encouraging update on his physical condition, insisting that his knee is no longer causing problems and that he is ready to compete for a more prominent role.

His comments arrive at an important moment for Flick, with Barcelona preparing to face Atletico Madrid this weekend and uncertainty emerging over the availability of fellow midfielder Rodri.

Speaking to reporters following the victory over Aston Villa, De Jong acknowledged that the speculation surrounding him had become a recurring source of frustration.

"There is always a bit of noise around me, especially when I'm not there," the midfielder said, as quoted by Mundo Deportivo.

"For some reason, in the summer when there is no football, or when I'm injured..."

De Jong went on to suggest that he receives more attention than other members of the Barcelona squad, although he accepted that this might partly reflect his personal perception.

"I feel that there is always more noise around me than around others. But perhaps that's just my impression when people talk about me. These are things I don't have much control over."

The Dutchman's remarks follow several months of questions about his fitness and position at Camp Nou.

De Jong sustained a knee ligament injury while representing the Netherlands at the 2026 World Cup, leaving him unavailable for the opening weeks of Barcelona's campaign.

During his recovery, he also addressed reports concerning his relationship with Flick and the club's hierarchy, rejecting suggestions that he had fallen out with the German coach.

His latest comments suggest that the scrutiny remains a sensitive subject, even after his return to competitive action.

Nevertheless, De Jong was keen to emphasise that his physical condition has improved considerably.

"I feel very good, very strong, with a lot of desire and motivation," he explained.

"My knee isn't bothering me. What's happened has happened."

The midfielder made his first appearance of the season as a substitute against Real Madrid on October 25, before starting Barcelona's emphatic 6-1 league victory over Alaves six days later.

Flick deployed him in central defence against Alaves, illustrating the versatility that has allowed the former Ajax player to operate in several positions during his Barcelona career.

Tuesday's Champions League encounter brought another significant moment, as De Jong replaced Pedri in the 70th minute to make his 300th competitive appearance for the club.

He was subsequently presented with a commemorative shirt by his teammates.

Having already surpassed Philip Cocu and Ronald Koeman, De Jong holds the record for the most competitive appearances by a Dutch player in Barcelona's history.

Despite his willingness to help in defence, De Jong made no secret of his preferred position or his desire to return to the starting lineup.

"I feel more comfortable in midfield, but I will help the team wherever it needs me," he said.

Asked whether he hoped to start against Atletico Madrid on Sunday, the Dutch international acknowledged that his recent injury would influence Flick's decision.

"I would like to start matches, but we have to take into account that I've been out for a long time, and that depends on the coaching staff."

His comments are particularly relevant following Rodri's withdrawal at half time against Aston Villa.

The former Manchester City midfielder complained of muscular discomfort during Tuesday's encounter, prompting Flick to introduce Marc Bernal for the second half.

Barcelona have since confirmed a minor hamstring injury, leaving Rodri's availability for the trip to Atletico in doubt.

Flick has already suggested that Bernal could be considered as a replacement, while also pointing out that De Jong has only recently returned from injury.

That leaves the Barcelona manager with a decision to make over his midfield combination, particularly given the demands of facing one of La Liga's strongest opponents away from home.

De Jong's ability to operate alongside Pedri, provide defensive cover or contribute further forward gives Flick another option, but his limited minutes this season remain a consideration.

The Dutchman has repeatedly expressed his commitment to Barcelona, having signed a contract extension until June 2029.

His immediate priority, however, is considerably more straightforward: putting his injury problems behind him and earning a place in the team through his performances.

After reaching 300 appearances, De Jong is determined that his next chapter at Barcelona should be defined by what he contributes on the pitch rather than the speculation surrounding him.

#2277. AEK Athens 2-3 Real Madrid: Los Blancos create Champions League history courtesy of Federico Valverde stunner in Athens

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 20:20, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 20:22

Real Madrid created a unique piece of Champions League history in Wednesday's 3-2 victory over AEK Athens in the league stage of the tournament.

Los Blancos twice needed to come from behind to level the scores in Athens, with Jude Bellingham making it 1-1 in the 32nd minute before Kylian Mbappe levelled it at 2-2 in the 78th minute, and it did appear that the points would be shared.

However, Federico Valverde had other ideas, coming up with a stunning goal in the 92nd minute to secure a valuable three points for the capital giants.

Real Madrid have now scored 49 Champions League goals in stoppage time, which is the most in the history of the competition.

"It was a very tough and complicated match, just as we expected. I think we imposed our control, but we had difficulty in creating chances. I'd like to congratulate the team on their efforts and hard work; let's continue working in this manner," Valverde told reporters following the victory in the league stage of the tournament.

Real Madrid remain perfect in the league stage of this season's Champions League, with Jose Mourinho's side claiming 12 points from four matches.

Los Blancos head coach Mourinho was full of praise for his team's performance.

"People often tend to say that the stronger team didn't start the match well, but to be honest, I felt that the team was fantastic," Mourinho told reporters.

"They delivered a performance that exceeded their usual capabilities, and they showed their best levels with high intensity from the very first minute, whether in winning duels, competing for balls, or executing attacking transitions."

When asked about Valverde, Mourinho added: "Fede has that ability. When he plays at right-back, it's hard for him to get into that kind of position.

"Today I didn't ask him to shoot, I told him the match was complicated. He was ready to play at high speed, both teams were tired, and a new player like Fede has the ability to run. I asked him to get into space."

Real Madrid have now posted seven straight wins in all competitions, and they will be bidding to make it eight in a row when they continue their La Liga campaign against Valencia on Saturday.

#2276. AC Milan 3-1 Inter: Five things we learned – individual and tactical success

AC Milan clinched a massive victory in their home derby against Inter on Saturday, with several positives to take from the game as a result.

There wasn’t exactly an excess of optimism before the game among the Milan fans, following what was a poor mid-week defeat and a so-so start to the season. However, we didn’t see much of this in the big game, with two goals scored after just ten minutes.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

It’s almost as if Gerry Cardinale manifested the win before the game and he certainly got to celebrate afterwards, even brought to tears in the mixed zone. He probably learnt a lot from this game and so did we, with several positives to highlight.

Without Diego Moreira, Adrien Rabiot and Mario Gila, Milan and Ruben Amorim struggled a lot in the mid-week clash against Bologna. Fortunately, the trio was back against Inter and the difference was clear as night and day.

All three played a big role, but the true difference-maker was Moreira, who was elctric from the start after bagging an early and lovely assist for Christian Pulisic. Six minutes later, in the 10th minute, he picked up a second from a corner kick, setting up Rabiot.

Inter got back into the game a little with their goal, making it 2-1, and it was a delicate moment in the game when Milan needed to stay focused. And yet again, it was Moreira who brought them out of trouble with a fantastic free-kick just before half-time.

San Siro erupted and the Belgian certainly showed that he has several tricks up his sleeve. On top of the attacking contribution, the Belgian was relentless throughout the game as he helped a tonne defensively until the final whistle.

Milan seem to have a game changer in their hands and it was fitting that he delivered this type of performance in his first Derby Della Madonnina. Especially since Ricky Kaka was in the stands, the player who made Moreira choose the No.22 shirt.

Milan did an outstanding job up front, being as clinical as they could be, but Inter were always going to create chances on the other end. So, it was even more impressive how the Rossoneri managed to contain them, especially in the second half.

There were some struggling moments in the first half, but in that period, Mike Maignan came up big with multiple good saves. One of them was just phenomenal, as he had little to no time to react and it was all about the Frenchman’s reflexes, which didn’t let him down.

That was a crucial moment in the game, with the result being 2-1 for Milan and with an equaliser there, the game could’ve been a lot different.

In the second half, the Rossoneri were a lot more compact and it was more about the players in front of Maignan. Strahinja Pavlovic stepped up a tonne after the half-time mark as he was a tad shaky in the opening 45 minutes, especially against Spence who was bursting down that flank.

Gila was also very solid on the right-hand side and especially when he was on the ball. It’s a completely different team when he’s on the pitch.

Diawara, Musah and Modric also did a very fine job closing down the spaces and handling Inter’s press well, with the Croatian rolling the years back as he was relentless throughout the game.

It was a very impressive defensive performance and especially in the second half of the game, as it seems Amorim’s instructions at the break limited Inter even further.

I purposefully left out Koni De Winter from the previous point because I think the Belgian deserves a section all for himself.

In the absence of Lautaro, Thuram was always going to be the main threat. Not just because he has five goals and four assists in the opening eight games, but also because he has done very well in the derby before.

So naturally, if Milan were to win the game, they had to neutralise the Frenchman and it was clear from the beginning that De Winter’s instructions were to stay close to the striker and not give him breathing space.

De Winter deserves all the praise here because he won almost all of the duels against the striker, effectively frustrating him and getting him out of the game completely. The only memory I have from Thuram was when he dropped almost into his own half towards the end of the game to try and make something happen, but that was not nearly enough.

Aside from his battle with the Frenchman, De Winter also did well with his passing and overall game and was arguably the best Milan defender on the night.

Really positive performance and he needs to keep it up, because conceding silly goals has been a problem for Milan and they clearly have the quality to prevent them.

We already mentioned the brilliant work at the back and the saves Maignan made, but it’s only fair to praise the attack too as they also did a fine job. Milan had three shots on goal until the 90th minute and had scored three goals, which is impressive considering how wasteful the Rossoneri have been in attack in the last few games.

The team started off with the right mindset and put pressure on Inter early on, with some great individual runs such as the one from Moreira. All of the chances were finished with clinical precision, which is exactly what you need to win in the big games.

We already praised Moreira, but Pulisic was also very good as he found the empty space on the opener and buried it to give his side the lead. He then continued playing well between the lines and he was smart about controlling the game after his side was ahead.

Rabiot came in clutch again with yet another goal and a positive performance overall, even if he had to do a bit more running in the second half and focus on the defensive aspect of the game.

I know he failed to score or assist again, but that doesn’t mean Goncalo Ramos was necessarily bad. He pressed quite a bit and he opened up spaces for his teammates. You can clearly see on Milan’s second goal how he pushes Sucic towards the byline so he can’t charge towards Rabiot after Moreira’s corner.

Again, he absolutely needs to be more involved in the box but he’s still a very valuable player on the pitch.

There was obviously a bittersweet feeling coming into the derby after losing to Bologna in such a frustrating manner, but the availability of Moreira, Rabiot and Gila gave Amorim and the fans some enthusiasm prior to the game.

To Amorim’s credit, he picked the correct starting eleven and his team had the right mindset from the start. The coach was aware of Inter’s fragile defending and instructed his team to raise the intensity from the start and apply pressure, which worked a treat with two goals in the opening ten minutes.

Inter then tried to come back into the game and created a few chances, it’s fair to say, but Amorim again tweaked something at the half-time break. His team was more compact and more aggressive after that, which almost completely neutralised Inter.

The coach also waited with his subs, which I think was correct given the team was playing well and there weren’t obvious signs of fatigue. When the changes did come, I think they were spot on.

More importantly, the whole game management was impressive. The game started off with Milan playing in Amorim’s style, which resulted in the goals but also exposed his team and then in the second half, Milan were more compact and not eager to press or attack as much.

On top of that, there were numerous times when the Rossoneri kept the ball, were patient and avoided risk and I think the tempo was dictated by Milan despite Inter having a bit more possession towards the end. Excellent from the coach.

#2275. Chelsea's hidden gem: the 19-year-old nobody saw coming

By Naim Beneddra | 04 Nov 2026 20:21

The 19-year-old defender has been included in Graham Potter's squad for November's UEFA Nations League fixtures against Bosnia and Herzegovina and Poland, marking another important step in his development.

Antwi joined Chelsea's sister club Strasbourg on a season-long loan in August, seeking the regular first-team opportunities that had been difficult to obtain at Stamford Bridge.

His progress in Ligue 1 has been impressive, with the Swedish full-back starting eight of Strasbourg's nine league matches since arriving in France.

And his performances have not gone unnoticed by Potter, the former Chelsea manager, who revealed that he had also received positive feedback about the youngster from people associated with the London club.

Truppen till avgörandet i Nations League ? ??? Bosnien-Hercegovina Stockholm | 14 november ??? Polen Wroclaw | 17 nov https://t.co/PbV8lwkQZ3

Antwi is one of two uncapped players selected by Potter for Sweden's upcoming Nations League double-header, alongside Fiorentina forward Alieu Njie.

The defender's inclusion follows a series of encouraging performances in France, where he has quickly established himself in Hugo Oliveira's plans.

Potter explained that an injury to Bologna right-back Emil Holm had created an opportunity to assess Antwi at senior international level.

"Genesis has done well with the Under-21 national team too, and with Emil Holm injured, I've chosen Felix Beijmo as the more experienced option," Potter explained during Wednesday's squad announcement, as reported by Swedish outlet Fotbollskanalen.

"Then we have Genesis, with his qualities and his pace."

The former Brighton & Hove Albion boss also revealed that he had spoken to people familiar with Antwi's development at Chelsea.

"I've spoken to people I know at Chelsea, and they think highly of him," Potter added.

That assessment is particularly encouraging for the Blues, who have invested several years in the defender's development.

Born in Stockholm, Antwi moved to England when he was 11 and joined Chelsea's academy, progressing through the age groups at Cobham before earning his first senior opportunities.

He made his competitive debut for the London club against Copenhagen in the Conference League in March 2025 and subsequently featured against Djurgarden in the same competition.

Chelsea rewarded his progress with a new contract in September 2025, tying him to the club until 2028, with an option to extend the agreement for another two years.

However, the decision to send him to Strasbourg this summer has provided the youngster with a considerably greater level of senior experience.

Antwi initially featured at left-back in the French club's opening league defeat against Marseille before moving to the right side of the defence.

His versatility and athleticism have subsequently helped him retain a regular place in Oliveira's side, including appearances against high-profile opponents such as Paris Saint-Germain and Monaco.

Potter acknowledged the value of that experience, praising the youngster's development during his first extended spell in senior football.

According to the Swedish Football Association, the national team manager believes that Antwi's performances at such a young age make him an exciting prospect to assess during the international break.

Antwi's first senior international call-up represents a significant milestone for a player who had previously represented Sweden at youth level, including five appearances for the Under-21 side.

It also provides Chelsea with an encouraging example of how their partnership with Strasbourg can accelerate the development of promising academy players.

The French club recruited five players from Chelsea during the summer transfer window, although their experiences have varied considerably.

Antwi, goalkeeper Filip Jorgensen and midfielder Dario Essugo have all established themselves as regular contributors.

Jorgensen has started nine league matches, making 32 saves, while Essugo has contributed two goals and an assist in seven appearances.

By contrast, former Chelsea youngsters Deivid Washington and Omari Kellyman, who joined Strasbourg on permanent deals, have barely featured.

Washington has yet to make a competitive appearance this season, while Kellyman has played only six minutes.

Those differences underline the varied experiences of players arriving in France through the two clubs' shared ownership structure.

For Antwi, however, the move has already delivered an important personal breakthrough.

Sweden will host Bosnia and Herzegovina on November 14 before travelling to face Poland three days later, with Potter's side leading their Nations League group.

A victory against Bosnia would secure promotion to League A, giving the Chelsea youngster the opportunity to be involved in an important international occasion.

Antwi has not yet made his senior Sweden debut, but his selection provides recognition of the progress he has made since leaving Stamford Bridge in search of regular football.

For Chelsea, the next stage will be monitoring whether he can maintain that development throughout the remainder of his loan spell and return to London with a stronger case for first-team opportunities.

#2274. Julien Stephan has two replacement options with 25-year-old sure to drop from QPR starting XI vs Lincoln City: Opinion

QPR face Lincoln City this weekend as they look to enter the international break on a high. The Hoops will welcome another home game, and they will be eager to put on a better show than they did in their midweek draw against Burnley.

Julien Stephan shared his frustration post-game, as his R’s side recorded 1.73 xG to the Clarets’ measly 0.16 (BBC Sport), yet the points were still shared. The French boss’ feelings were in line with those of supporters, but amid a strong campaign to date, there is confidence that Rangers can return to form.

Stephan is bound to make changes from the Burnley tie, and Koki Saito is highly likely to drop out. If that does prove to be the case, there are two QPR players in strong contention to replace him.

Saito was making his first start since September 2nd in the Burnley draw. He has dealt with injury, limiting him to brief appearances off the bench and keeping him out of the Hoops’ matchday squad completely on three occasions.

It was a tough return for the 25-year-old. He created two chances for an expected assists of 0.25, but 33 touches ranked him 9th of QPR’s 11 starters, and his hour-mark replacement Ilias Chair came on to register an assist. He also created the same amount of chances and had only three less touches in half the time (FotMob).

Despite this, Chair seems unlikely to be considered for a start. The R’s favourite is only two games into his injury comeback and Stephan has said he is more likely to come into contention for a return to the first XI after taking the international break to build his fitness.

The same goes for Karamoko Dembele, who has been coming on off the bench after his ACL injury. As such, the two players most likely to replace Saito are Kwame Poku or Rayan Kolli.

Poku would likely start on the right, seeing Harvey Vale shifted over to the left. He was not used at all in midweek, so would be fit and ready to come in from the start.

As for Kolli, he would join Richard Kone in the frontline while Paul Smyth reverts to his more natural wing role. The Northern Irishman has been a key player this season with three goals and three assists in 13 Championship matches.

Stephan has a strong squad at his disposal, despite some key QPR men such as Nicolas Madsen and Rumarn Burrell remaining sidelined. It means he has plenty of scope for change in the bid to get back to winning ways.

It has been a positive season to date, with the Hoops sitting 11th in the table and two points off the Championship play-offs. Despite this, a result here feels important.

Failure to win would make it one victory in the last five Championship matches. Lincoln City are a beatable opponent too, as shown by five defeats and only one victory in their last seven in the league.

The Imps are a frustrating side to break down, but QPR have shown in big wins that at their best – like in big wins over Derby County and Swansea City – they can be one of the most ruthless attacking sides in the league.

#2273. Fenerbahce 3-2 Liverpool: Reds head coach Andoni Iraola explains "the main reason" for his team's Champions League defeat

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 20:41, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 20:45

Liverpool head coach Andoni Iraola has bemoaned his team's defending in Wednesday's 3-2 defeat to Turkish giants Fenerbahce in the Champions League.

Twice Liverpool led in Turkey, with Trey Nyoni sending the Reds ahead in the 25th minute, before Alexander Isak made it 2-1 in the 69th minute.

However, Fenerbahce managed to level it up at 2-2 in the 73rd minute, before former Mallorca striker Vedat Muriqi won it for the hosts six minutes later.

Liverpool have now lost back-to-back matches under Iraola for the first time, having also gone down 1-0 to Arsenal in the Premier League on Sunday, and the club's manager was unhappy with the manner of the defending in the European fixture.

Liverpool have lost back-to-back games under Andoni Iraola for the first time: 1-0 vs. Arsenal ❌ 3-2 vs. Fenerbahçe ❌ https://t.co/vUHFRJF1ob

"The main reason that I can find for why we lost this game is we conceded from three set pieces. It's something that we were doing very well in the league, we were doing quite well until today," Iraola told reporters in his post-match press conference.

"We knew it was one of the strengths of Fenerbahce. We concede from a free-kick, a corner and even from the long throw at the end.

"They're goals that have hurt us because we've gone twice in front after having good moments, controlling the game, arriving, we've found the goals but after we've conceded in situations we should definitely defend better.

"It's frustration because it's a difficult place to come here and play. In most parts of the game we were thinking we could win this one but obviously it hasn't happened.

"Especially the first goal and the third goal - the second one is a rebound - but the first one, I don't think we set the wall properly and the last one, Muriqi wins the ball against three defenders, it's definitely something we need to defend better."

#2272. Celtic AGM latest as O'Neill talks long-term future and tense CSC visit

For Martin O'Neill, it has already been a hectic week. On Monday evening, he attended a meeting at Greenock Celtic Supporters' Club alongside interim chairman Brian Wilson, chief executive Michael Nicholson, and chief financial officer Chris McKay. Over 120 fans were present as the Celtic manager and board faced, according to the club, 'hard but fair' questioning.

Less than 24 hours later, O'Neill found himself in Dingwall with assistant coaches Mark Fotheringham and Stephen McManus watching Celtic B lose 2-0 to Ross County in the Scottish Challenge Cup.

That's all before two crucial home fixtures against Besiktas in the Europa League and Kilmarnock in the William Hill Premiership come into the fray.

The 74-year-old sat down ahead of the tie with Besiktas to discuss Monday's meeting, his long-term future, and the club's upcoming Annual General Meeting (AGM).

"First of all, I wasn't really aware of the meeting, so it was kind of an ad hoc thing," O'Neill explained. "But I obviously was around. I spoke to Michael [Nicholson] and he said that they were going to meet.

"And I said, well, I wouldn't mind going down to it if that was the case, obviously to experience what the fans would be asking.

"The meeting itself was, I think you could use the word robust, yes, firm, but very orderly questioning.

"And trying to give the answers that, not necessarily what people are looking for, because I'm not so sure that will ever reach a satisfactory stage for a while anyway. But, yeah, it was really interesting and an experience.

"I probably didn't really need to go to a meeting, but these people were big Celtic fans. And naturally, I think both board and supporters, I thought there was a wanting the best for the football club.

"At the end of the day, there are bad times and I think that you should face up to that. Was everything answered by the board and myself to their satisfaction? One, you'd have to ask the people there at the time what was there.

"There was no shouting. It wasn't like the AGM that appeared [last year]. The shouting had taken place before the meeting."

Indeed, as O'Neill and company arrived in Greenock, a number of Celtic supporters wearing balaclavas were outside the supporters' club, voicing their own concerns.

"To me, I think that was the saddest thing," O'Neill continued. "Would I have ever, in any day, ever thought that Celtic fans would be clothed in balaclavas before a meeting? Even if they were disapproving of what was happening. I think that's really disappointing.

"I do find it hard to get my head round that kind of stuff.

"I think if you've got a dispute or something, I think you should be brave enough to show your face doing it.

"[It was] just on the way in. I don't know whether they expected me to be there or not, but it doesn't really matter. It was just regardless of who was there."

The hope is that this year's AGM doesn't entail similar hostilities. The last iteration was adjourned early following animosity between the hierarchy and supporters.

It's understood the 2026 AGM will take place in early December in person.

"I would expect to be at it as a manager," O'Neill said. "So if given an opportunity to answer some of the things and if they want to hear from the manager, I would have no problem.

"I hope it's similar to what Monday night was in terms of the questions and fairness and not falling into what it was last year."

As O'Neill discusses myriad off-pitch matters, it's easy to forget he's Celtic's football manager.

"People are asking me now if it’s something I’m going to continue to do," he added. "No, my main job depends on results and winning some football matches. That’s the most important thing."

Contracted at Celtic Park until the end of this season, O'Neill's deal has the option of a further 12 months.

This time last year, he was two games into what was meant to be the most fleeting of interim spells.

Wilfried Nancy's disastrous reign came and went and O'Neill returned in January, winning the double and staying on as permanent manager in the summer.

When asked if this was his final season in charge, O'Neill responded: "I can’t imagine anybody asking me back. Seriously, I can’t imagine that happening.

"Listen, I didn’t come in here today to give a final word at this minute."

If the coming months are to be his last, how important is it to go out on a high?

"It would be nice to see the club that I knew intimately from 2000 – having followed from a distance since my school days – be successful.

"Did I ever think I’d see a day when 75,000 people would descend on a UEFA Cup final day, with no arrests? There are still pictures on the wall here and all you see is a sea of green and white. To see that again, that would be your dream."

Celtic, though, could not be further away from reaching another European final. Sunday's defeat to Falkirk was their sixth in 10 matches, with Besiktas up next in the Europa League.

From their first three fixtures in the competition, Celtic are pointless, heightening criticism on O'Neill and his coaches.

"Everyone is a tactician now, that’s the whole point," he said. "Everybody is a football manager. There are people who have got jobs in football now – at football clubs – because they played PlayStation Manager.

"Honestly, it’s extraordinary. But it does not bother me, honestly, it doesn’t. People talk about tactics but in my semi-defence, no-one talked about tactics when we won the seven games to win the league [last season]. Or the 14 games we won before we got beat.

"And you know what, you’re right to think – if you got beaten in a game – that maybe somebody has out-thought you. Absolutely. You’re right to think that.

"[Falkirk manager] John McGlynn has every right to be proud of his side after beating us and to have had the upper hand."

Despite recent form, O'Neill remains more determined than ever to turn things around.

"I'm supposed to be the driver of the club," he quipped.

That said, the 74-year-old is well aware of the threat Besiktas, who sit eighth in the 36-team Europa League table, pose.

"I think these games are really hard for us," O'Neill said. "I think there are teams in the Europa League who are capable of playing in the Champions League and getting results in the Champions League at this minute. However, we're in the Europa League.

"We haven't got a point on the board. We're going to try and do something about it if we can on Thursday and then ask the question.

"But I think that no-one has ever lost the first three and qualified for the competition. So, we’ll be up against it. We're definitely up against it. But are we capable? Yeah, I have to believe that."

#2271. GdS: An ‘achievable dream’ – why Cardinale left Italy ‘convinced’ by Scudetto bid

Gerry Cardinale left San Siro after the derby win over Inter with a distinct feeling: this AC Milan team can challenge for the title.

Happy as ever about the derby victory, yet still rueful over the defeat to Bologna – a stumble that must not be repeated – La Gazzetta dello Sport (seen below) write that Cardinale departed Italy yesterday, bound for New York, where his family and work await him.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

His thoughts however remain firmly fixed on AC Milan and the tasks that lie ahead, with the renewal of Adrien Rabiot’s contract as a short-term priority and the Scudetto as a cherished, long-term dream.

He admitted as much following the loss to Raffaele Palladino’s side, and he feels it even more strongly now, having won his first derby since taking direct personal control of the club on May 25th.

On Sunday evening, following the final whistle, Cardinale chose not to speak to the team. He had already said everything he needed to say to his players on Saturday at Milanello, aiming to motivate and spur them on to bounce back from the 4-2 defeat against Bologna.

The team took his words to heart: after the derby, Maignan and his team-mates could see the joy on Cardinale’s face, even though he didn’t utter a single word. Amorim, who granted the squad two days of (training set to resume tomorrow) offered his own praise for the team’s performance.

The head of RedBird Capital stepped away from the dressing room, wanting the moment to remain special for the players and the coaching staff, but also because he was deeply moved—tears nearly welling up in his eyes.

Milan, its fans, and the project itself all needed a night like this, and the American knew it. Now, however, Gerry expects the level of performance shown in the derby to be replicated in the upcoming matches, specifically the two before the break: Thursday against Ferencváros and Sunday in Genoa.

These are fixtures that will require managing the post-derby euphoria to ensure there is no drop in focus. While some of the squad celebrated at a well-known Milanese nightclub on Saturday night, the focus shifts to Europe starting tomorrow.

The position in the table needs to be improved following the slip-ups against Benfica and Bournemouth in the first three games. Beyond that, further progress will be required in Serie A.

The only way to close the seven-point gap on Lazio is to avoid any further stumbles like the one against Bologna. On this point, Cardinale and Amorim are in complete agreement.

In fact, while both were delighted by the triumph over their city rivals, that joy only sharpened their regret over the three points dropped against the Rossoblù. Neither the manager nor the owner could stomach the collapse from a 2-1 lead at halftime to a 4-2 defeat.

The two spoke after the derby and are fully aligned: injuries notwithstanding, a Milan side that aspires to be Milan once again – both in Italy and in Europe – cannot afford such lapses in focus.

Beating Inter is satisfying, and remaining unbeaten against their rivals for eight consecutive matches is a positive, but the ultimate priority remains lifting a trophy. City supremacy is not enough, not for Cardinale, and not for Amorim.

#2270. AEK Athens 2-3 Real Madrid: Struggling Vinicius Junior puts in another alarming performance for Los Blancos

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 21:07, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 21:09

Real Madrid head coach Jose Mourinho said that he was pleased with Vinicius Junior's performance in Wednesday's 3-2 win over AEK Athens in the Champions League.

Vinicius has not played in La Liga for almost a month, having been sent off in the team's contest with Villarreal at Bernabeu on October 10.

The Brazil international was again absent against Racing de Santander on Sunday, but he was brought back into the XI against AEK in the league stage of the Champions League.

Vinicius again found it difficult to make his mark and was replaced in the 81st minute of the contest, three minutes after Kylian Mbappe had made it 2-2.

Mourinho praised Vinicius after the match, telling reporters: "Vinicius Junior did very well, from my point of view, he put in a good performance. Later, of course, I preferred to inject fresh blood into the flanks by making two substitutions."

However, the numbers behind Vinicius' performance paint a different picture.

According to Sofascore, Vinicius lost possession on 11 occasions, while he only had 48 touches during his time on the field against the Greek club.

Vinicius managed three shots but was unable to hit the target, while only one of his three crosses in the Champions League fixture was accurate.

The defensive numbers behind Vinicius' performance are also alarming, having failed to provide a single defensive contribution, while he was dribbled past twice and only managed to win two his seven ground duels against AEK.

Vinicius has scored just once in 12 appearances for Real Madrid this season, in addition to coming up with three assists.

There had been a host of speculation surrounding Vinicius' future over the summer, with Arsenal making a strong play to sign the 26-year-old.

However, Vinicius ultimately signed a new long-term contract at Bernabeu.

The forward has scored 129 goals and registered 103 assists in 387 matches for Real Madrid during a highly-successful period, but there is no question that the South American is currently struggling for form at Bernabeu.

#2269. ‘Great news’: Birmingham City pair to return against Swansea City, says Chris Davies

Birmingham City are back in Championship action this weekend with a home clash against Swansea City. BCFC won 3-0 against Millwall last time out after goals by Alex Cochrane, Max Bird and Jay Stansfield.

Paik has been sidelined for the Blues since their clash against Middlesbrough back in mid-September. As for Fry, he has been ill recently but is now ready to play again now.

In this latest update regarding the duo, boss Davies has said, as per a report by BirminghamLive: “Paik is back training fully which is great news. He’s a fantastic player, a really good servant to the team, and we’ve been a little bit lighter in midfield for options so it’s good to have him back. Dael has been training for the last few days so he’s ready to go again.”

Birmingham City are sat up in 3rd place in the table after their first 14 fixtures behind Middlesbrough and Wolves. They are in the play-offs along with Stoke City, Swansea City, West Brom, Southampton and West Ham.

Birmingham City will be pleased to have Paik back. He gives BCFC more quality in the middle of the park.

The South Korea international, who has 30 caps to his name, has been a key player for the Blues since his move to England back in 2024 and he has played over 100 games for the Midlands outfit in all competitions. He battles it out with the likes of Jhon Solis, Tomoki Iwata and Bird for a place in Davies’ starting XI.

As for Fry, he provides competition and depth to Birmingham City’s defensive department. He joined them in the summer on a free transfer following the end of his contract at Middlesbrough.

However, he has struggled for minutes so far since his transfer and has had to bide his time for opportunities. Christoph Klarer and Phil Neumann have been rock solid and are ahead of him in the pecking order at this moment in time.

Birmingham City are unlikely to throw Paik and Fry into their starting team against Swansea City. BCFC are likely to put them on the bench and potentially bring them on.

The Blues won’t want to take any risks with either, especially Paik as he has been out for a while. Fry isn’t a regular starter anyway but his return is a boost as he offers strong cover in his position.

Davies’ men will be feeling confident right now after their victory over Millwall. They have the best defensive record in the division and are tough to break down.

#2268. Alfred Johansson issues major style warning to his Motherwell side

Motherwell manager Alfred Johansson believes they will need to develop different edges to their game as opponents catch up with their passing style.

Well are second only to Celtic in attempted passes and passing accuracy in the William Hill Premiership this season as they continue the brand of football introduced by Johansson’s predecessor, Jens Berthel Askou, last summer.

But other teams are now catching on after the success Motherwell and Falkirk had last season in finishing in the top six.

Johansson cited recent statistics that showed passes per Premiership game had increased by more than 100 to close to 900 from four seasons ago to this campaign, while passing accuracy was up from 74 per cent to more than 80 per cent and long passes had reduced by around a quarter over the same period.

“I saw some statistics a couple of weeks ago, how much higher the amount of passes in the Scottish football games are this season, compared only with last season,” Johansson said.

“There was also a comparison over a five-year period, and the amount of passes has increased a lot. And it was also a big step from last season to how this season has started.

“The game is moving in that direction, which I guess is natural. I mean, that’s how the trend has been in the rest of European football for quite some time.”

Johansson’s opponent on Saturday, Jim Goodwin, admitted last season that he would aspire to play the sort of football that Askou delivered and his new-look midfield trio of Dylan Tait, Lyall Cameron and Zidane Iqbal have helped adapt their style of play.

United are unbeaten in six matches since losing at Fir Park while Motherwell’s 1-0 win over St Mirren last weekend was their first in eight games.

Johansson senses that Motherwell’s style of play will not give them an inherent advantage.

“It was very different from many of the other teams (last season) and that then gave Motherwell an edge because they were very early,” the Swede said.

“Whereas this season, it’s more teams trying to, maybe not play in the exact same way, but at least have more passes than they had in the past.

“So if that is the case, that’s not going to be the one thing that’s going to be the difference between the teams.

“So I guess that style of play will not be the thing that separates the winner from loser. I think you’re going to have to be finding an edge elsewhere for how good you are with and without the ball.

“You need to be good on your basics. Making minor adaptations to an opponent is always going to be there, but it’s going to be minor changes.”

#2267. "Lost his spark..." - Vinicius Jr handed brutal verdict by Spanish media despite Real Madrid win

By Lewis Blain | 04 Nov 2026 21:32

Real Madrid maintained their perfect start in the 2026-27 Champions League league phase with a 3-2 victory over AEK Athens on Wednesday evening, but Vinicius Junior failed to take advantage of an opportunity to impress Jose Mourinho.

The Brazilian was handed a chance to strengthen his claims for a regular starting position ahead of his return from domestic suspension, but delivered another underwhelming performance on a night where Yan Diomande continued to enhance his reputation.

Vinicius's display attracted widespread criticism in Spain, with one publication awarding him just 2/10, while several prominent journalists questioned whether he deserves to remain in Madrid's strongest starting XI.

ElDesmarque were particularly scathing, awarding Vinicius just 2/10 and criticising his lack of attacking influence and defensive contribution. They highlighted his failure to track back, the space he left behind him and the fact that he completed none of his attempted dribbles.

AS were similarly unimpressed, describing his first half as ineffective and his second-half efforts as "un quiero y no puedo" - 'trying but unable to deliver'.

Former Real Madrid player and coach Jorge Valdano felt that "La palabra aceptable no es para un jugador como Vinicius" - 'the word acceptable does not apply to a player like Vinicius' - adding that he had produced just one good attacking move.

AS deputy editor Tomas Roncero was even more direct, declaring "Vini tenía una opción para lucirse, pero nada" - Vini had an opportunity to shine, but 'delivered nothing'. He subsequently questioned whether the Brazilian deserved to start, claiming that he had "perdido la luz" - 'lost his spark'.

Cadena SER's Dani Garrido agreed that Vinicius had wasted his opportunity, while Real Madrid correspondent Javier Herraez offered a pointed comparison with his rival for a starting position: "Diomande es un ejemplo, aprende de él Vini" - 'Diomande is an example, learn from him, Vini.'

The growing statistical gap between Vinicius and Diomande makes the criticism particularly difficult to dismiss.

According to figures highlighted by El Pais, Vinicius has attempted 37 dribbles this season, successfully completing just 15 at a rate of 40%. Diomande, meanwhile, has attempted 56 and completed 36, boasting a considerably better success rate of 64%.

Even more concerning is that Vinicius has gone two consecutive Champions League matches without attempting a single dribble, an extraordinary statistic for someone whose explosive running and ability to beat defenders have traditionally been his biggest strengths.

Diomande is therefore offering Mourinho an increasingly convincing alternative, with AS suggesting that the summer signing is already several lengths ahead in the battle for a starting position.

One disappointing performance would not normally threaten the status of a player with Vinicius's achievements, but the mounting criticism suggests that his problems are becoming a recurring issue rather than an isolated loss of form.

The Brazilian still possesses the quality to turn things around, but he needs to rediscover his confidence, directness and defensive commitment sooner rather than later.

With Diomande making a compelling case to remain in the team, Vinicius can no longer rely solely on his reputation to guarantee a starting position at the Bernabeu.

#2266. CorSera: Inacio then Tresoldi – Milan’s plans to level up the squad outlined

The derby victory against Inter has brought a sense of excitement to AC Milan, and plans are being drawn up for a potentially busy January.

This morning’s edition of Corriere della Sera (via MilanNews) writes that Gerry Cardinale was among the happiest in attendance on Saturday night. The RedBird boss continues to reiterate his desire to bring Milan back to the top, and for this reason he’s ready to strengthen the team in January.

Not only that, but he is ready and willing to make signings that make a guaranteed difference in terms of quality, unlike last winter when the only reinforcement to arrive was Niclas Füllkrug from West Ham (now the Bundesliga top scorer).

The first move is the hiring of Markus Krosche, who will officially become the new Head of Football in the coming weeks. Milan lack someone in this role, having left it vacant since the failed summer pursuit of the German, but Cardinale didn’t give up and has got his man.

In January, the priority remains a central defender, and Gonçalo Inacio’s name remains at the top of the list. The Rossoneri are ready to try again for the Sporting CP centre-back, who – like Krösche – was pursued in the summer too.

Then there’s the mystery of Ramos’s replacement in attack, as Francesco Camarda is struggling to find playing time. So it’s possible the young striker could be sent out on loan to play consistently and continue his development.

A new centre-forward would replace him, alternating with Gonçalo Ramos. The dream would be Nicolò Tresoldi, but bringing him to Milan in the winter transfer window seems nearly impossible. The matter will likely be discussed again in the summer.

#2265. Man United must move heaven and earth to bring Cesc Fabregas to Old Trafford as their next head coach

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 21:56, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 22:03

Como put in one of the most extraordinary performances in Champions League history on Wednesday, with the Italian side recording a 9-1 win over Lens.

Anastasios Douvikas struck a hat-trick, while Nico Paz, Lucas Da Cunha, Marc Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and Matteo Papaccioli were also on the scoresheet in France.

Como beat Manchester United 4-1 in their previous game in the Champions League, and Wednesday's success has left them fourth in the overall table, picking up nine points from four matches.

The Blue and Whites have netted 19 times in the league stage of the 2026-27 Champions League, only the holders Paris Saint-Germain (20) have more.

Only two teams have scored eight or more goals in a single Champions League fixture over the last 10 seasons, with Como now on an elite list with Bayern Munich - 8-2 vs. Barcelona in 2020 and then 9-2 vs. Dinamo Zagreb in 2024.

Meanwhile, Como are the first team to score nine in an away European Cup match since Feyenoord did so against Olympiakos Nicosia in September 1971.

The team's fourth-placed finish in the 2025-26 Serie A campaign saw them qualify for the Champions League, and they have already shown that competing at this level will not be an issue.

I Biancoblu have some incredible players - notably the excellent Paz - but so much praise has to go to head coach Cesc Fabregas, who is already making serious waves in the management game.

Fabregas, who will turn 40 next May, has won 46, drawn 26 and lost 25 of his 97 matches in charge of Como since being appointed permanent head coach in July 2024.

Como are fourth in the Serie A table this season on 23 points, only five points behind the leaders Lazio, and a challenge for the title cannot be ruled out.

Fabregas has a contract at Como until the summer of 2028, but Man United should make an attempt to appoint the Spaniard as their new head coach next year.

Whether that means keeping Michael Carrick and enduring some more pain is a different question entirely; there is also the option of bringing in an interim head coach for the rest of the season, should the club ultimately decide to move away from Carrick.

Fabregas' Arsenal and Chelsea connections would make a move to Man United a fascinating one, but he knows the Premier League and also knows what it takes to win it, doing so twice with Chelsea.

The Spaniard was a wonderfully gifted midfielder and has shown during his early management career that he can put together a team capable of playing incredibly exciting football.

Como were head and shoulders above the Red Devils in their recent European clash.

Man United must look to the future with their next appointment, and Fabregas has the potential to become one of the leading coaches in world football in the coming years.

#2264. Blackburn Rovers need to target Liverpool player again in January after summer transfer interest: Opinion

Blackburn Rovers will get the chance to bring in some new faces this winter ahead of the second-half of the season. They are currently positioned in 14th place in the Championship table after 14 fixtures and they are five points off the play-offs behind West Ham in 8th.

Rovers were credited with an interest in Liverpool left-back Luke Chambers over the summer along with fellow second tier teams Portsmouth and QPR. However, nothing materialised in the end and he stayed put at Anfield beyond the deadline in early September.

Tony Mowbray’s side should rekindle their pursuit of him now though and offer him the prospect of regular game time in the EFL. The Preston-born man has made one appearance for the Reds in this campaign in the Champions League and is understandably down the pecking order with the Premier League due to stiff competition for his position.

Blackburn Rovers could provide him with consistent minutes at Ewood Park. He needs a platform to show more of what he can do as opposed to sitting on Liverpool’s bench.

Chambers would give Blackburn Rovers more competition and depth in their defensive department. He can offer Rovers another option and something different to what they already have.

The ex-England youth international likes to get up and down the left flank of defence and get involved in attacks. He has been on the books of Liverpool for his whole career to date and and has made five outings for their first-team.

Chambers has also been shipped out on loan to Kilmarnock, Wigan Athletic and Charlton Athletic to get some experience and to boost his development. He played 18 times for the Addicks last term under Nathan Jones during his time at The Valley.

If he was acquired by Blackburn Rovers, he would battle it out with Harry Pickering and Yuri Ribeiro for a place in Mowbray‘s starting XI. At 22, he has potential to get better down the line.

Blackburn Rovers have enjoyed success with past Liverpool loan stars like Harvey Elliott and Owen Beck in the past. Chambers could be the next Reds man to link up with Rovers and catch the eye.

The Lancashire outfit need to ensure that they get their recruitment right in January. They need young and hungry players who are willing to fight for the cause as they push for a place in the top eight against the odds.

#2262. Wednesday's Champions League roundup: Como make Champions League history as Real Madrid leave it late

By Ellis Stevens | 04 Nov 2026 22:22

The UEFA Champions League took centre stage once again on Wednesday, with nine league phase fixtures taking place on matchday four of the 2026-27 campaign.

European heavyweights Real Madrid and Liverpool got the action underway in the early kick-offs against AEK Athens and Fenerbahce respectively, followed by seven matches later in the night.

Como equalled history in the standout match of the night, defeating Lens 9-1 away from home, while Arsenal, Porto, Borussia Dortmund, Manchester City, Club Brugge and Viking all also secured three points.

Here, Sports Mole provides a roundup of Wednesday's Champions League action.

Como make history, equalling the record for most goals scored in a single Champions League game ? On his senior debut, 18-year-old Matteo Papaccioli makes it 9-1 against Lens ? ? Stream now on TNT Sports & HBO Max https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB https://t.co/TISehVKIqD

Como delivered the standout result of the night as they secured a sensational 9-1 victory over Lens, equalling the most goals scored in a single Champions League match.

Como raced out of the blocks as Anastasios Douvikas opened the scoring after seven minutes, and the early goal set the tempo for a nine-goal thriller in the Champions League.

Douvikas added a second in the first-half before sealing his hat-trick just before the hour mark, with goals elsewhere from Nico Paz, Lucas De Cunha, Marc Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali and Martin Baturina making it 8-1 heading into the final minutes, with Ruben Aguilar netting a consolation.

Debutant Matteo Papaccioli then made history in the final minutes, scoring Como's ninth goal to equal the record for most goals scored in a single Champions League match.

The victory means Como have nine points from their first three Champions League games, leaving them fourth in the standings, while Lens fall out of the qualifying places to 33rd.

Federico Valverde smashed home a stunning late winner to seal a dramatic 3-2 victory for Real Madrid against AEK Athens, extending Los Blancos' flawless league phase campaign.

Jose Mourinho's side, who have now scored five match-winning goals in the final five minutes of games this season, twice came from behind before Valverde secured their fourth consecutive league phase victory this season.

Aboubakary Koita and Stavros Pilios each gave AEK Athens the lead, only for Real Madrid to pull back level on both occasions thanks to goals from Jude Bellingham and Kylian Mbappe.

Real Madrid then left it late to claim the victory, with Valverde rifling a long-range effort into the top corner in the 93rd minute, breaking AEK Athens' hearts.

The hosts' night continued to sour after the full-time whistle, with Koita, who had both scored and assisted, receiving a red card following continued protests towards the referee.

The defeat leaves AEK Athens with three points from four league phase fixtures, after one win and three defeats, while Real Madrid climb to second in the standings with four wins from four outings.

Trey Nyoni and Alexander Isak had both put Liverpool ahead earlier in the game, only for Fenerbahce to respond within four minutes on each occasion, first through Mason Greenwood's deflected effort and then via Archie Brown's strike.

The result saw Andoni Iraola's Liverpool suffer their first defeat of the league phase, while Fenerbahce claimed their first victory in the 2026-27 Champions League.

Noni Madueke's early goal gave Arsenal the lead before Viktor Gyokeres brilliantly doubled the advantage just before half-time.

Slavia Prague controlled much of the second half as they searched for a way back into the game, and the hosts made it a nervy finish as Wiktor Nowak pulled one back in the 90th minute.

However, the Gunners managed to defend the late pressure and escape with the 2-1 win.

A third victory from four league phase matches lifts Arsenal to third in the standings, while Slavia Prague's search for a first Champions League victory this season continues, with the hosts falling to 34th in the table.

#2261. Pioli discusses current Milan team, relationship with Ibrahimovic, Scudetto win and Moreira

Stefano Pioli shared some more insight on his time as AC Milan head coach, including the impact of Zlatan Ibrahimovic, Rafael Leao and others.

Pioli was a guest on Sky Calcio Club after Juventus 2-0 Napoli last night, and he revisited the 2021-22 Scudetto win, discussing some of the characteristics of the players, and also drawing comparisons with some current squad members. MilanVibes relayed his words.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Firstly, the coach was asked about if he saw a bit of Rafael Leao in Diego Moreira, given the Belgian was the hero of the derby on Saturday night with one goal and two assists.

“I saw him against Sassuolo too, and he impressed me. He and Rabiot are really strong. Milan played a great match, Maignan made a great save… but with their starting eleven, they’re a really strong team. When Amorim made a few more substitutions, they struggled more.

“It took them a while to get to this point because they had players arriving, probably with some problems, and it takes time to find their rhythm and fitness.

“The results are showing. Modric also still has some important strength. Gonçalo Ramos is doing a great job for the team and is a very good player.”

The discussion about Milan inevitably brought them back to the team that won the league title in 2022. Pioli explained how the playing system was built around the characteristics of the players available, with particular attention to the defensive line and the ability to sustain aggressive football.

“A coach’s skill lies in adapting to a player’s characteristics. At AC Milan, I had players who got lost defending deep. I like that style of play, but it was the best one for those players.

“Kalulu and Tomori also allowed us to take risks. If you want to stay high up the pitch, you need to have significant intensity; anything in between doesn’t work.”

Pioli commented on Leao again, who reached the peak of his powers under the Italian, but also admitted that after the Scudetto not everything went as he would have hoped.

The Scudetto year was devastating. I’ve seen few players of that level. Then he wasn’t, and we weren’t, good. Because when you win, it’s because something special is created in the team. Paolo [Maldini], Ricky [Massara], and I weren’t good – and neither was he – at maintaining and further raising his level.

“He certainly has important qualities. The coach has to understand the players. Rafa is a good player who needs to be encouraged in a certain way.”

During the segment, the background on Roma’s interest also emerged. The Giallorossi contacted Pioli, but at that point the coach had already received an offer from Al Nassr.

“There were times when everyone called me. Even Roma, but I had just received my contract with Al Nassr and I couldn’t break my word.

“I don’t regret going to Saudi Arabia. I wanted to gain experience abroad after my years with AC Milan. The financial aspect and the opportunity to coach Cristiano Ronaldo also played a role.

“If you don’t coach him, you don’t understand how great he is. He’s a champion, obsessed with preparing to score goals. He’s very respectful.”

Pioli spoke about Zlatan Ibrahimovic and Cristiano Ronaldo, two of the biggest profiles that he has had to manage in his career to date.

“Cristiano and Zlatan have a very similar relationship: ‘You’re clear with me and I respect you, if you’re not clear with me, it’s no good.’ I’ve always based our relationship on clarity and sincerity.

“It’s clear that with Zlatan and Cristiano, if you tell them, ‘Look, you’re not playing tomorrow, or you’ll play 60 minutes tomorrow,’ and then I’ll take you off, and you do it or you don’t, it makes a difference.

“I didn’t tell Zlatan something once, but I didn’t tell him because in the rush, I think during the Covid period, we were playing every three days. At the end of the first half it was 0-0, then in 10 minutes we scored 2-3 goals, I don’t remember now…

“Anyway, I made three substitutions: I remember Zlatan, Calhanoglu, and another. So quick, but I hadn’t told Zlatan in advance… The next day [laughs]. he came to me… The good thing about Zlatan is that as soon as he gets over it, the next day it’s as if nothing had happened.”

When asked if Ibrahimovic frequently sulked, Pioli replied: “No, absolutely not. He’s great. But does he tell you? Yes, yes, and then with a tone…

“I always leave my office door open because I like to greet the players and see them. In fact, I had the office changed because I wanted it to be between the locker room and physiotherapy, which they have to go to every day anyway, so every day I greeted all the players, and they came in to see me.

“Every now and then I stopped them, but I didn’t stop them. That day, I didn’t expect Zlatan to come in. He came in and said, ‘I’ll speak today.’ You know, those chairs that move around, I danced for 2-3 minutes, but then it went away.”

Pioli also recalled his first meeting with the Swede, who arrived at Milanello at the end of January 2020 and undoubtedly played a key role in the resurgence.

“Ibrahimovic arrived at Milanello the first day. I waited for him there, and he arrived when it was dark. It was January, the end of January. I followed him in his training, but he also had a knee problem.

“He did a bit of treadmill work, then he came to my office, spoke to me a bit, and I explained to him how I wanted to use him on the pitch, technically and tactically. He said to me: ‘You’re the coach, I’m the player. You respect me, I’ll respect you.’ And that’s what happened.”

Pioli also spoke about the relationship between results and the team, explaining why it’s often more difficult for a coach to put a defeat behind him than to enjoy a victory.

“We coaches don’t enjoy victories because we immediately think about the next game. Defeat, however, stays with you. But what happened to me at Milan was incredibly emotional. Sackings, on the other hand, are difficult; you feel guilty.

“The one in Florence was even more difficult. I had returned with so much willpower and energy. I had returned with so much confidence, and it was a tough blow.”

Finally, a revelation about the possibility of becoming Italy’s coach. Pioli confirmed that he had been close to taking over the national team, but without going into details.

“I’ve touched on it, but not for very long. In our careers, we are the product of all the choices we make. Some are good, some aren’t.”

#2260. Philadelphia Union vs Toronto - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joel Lefevre | 04 Nov 2026 22:38

The Philadelphia Union can lock up sixth place in the Eastern Conference of Major League Soccer with a win on Saturday as they conclude the 2026 regular season against Toronto at Subaru Park.

A 1-0 defeat at FC Cincinnati on Halloween has Philly just a point above the latter in the East heading into Decision Day, while Toronto were eliminated from post-season contention following a 3-1 defeat at Atlanta United.

A coaching change dramatically turned the Union’s fortunes around this season, as they became the first MLS side to make the playoffs after earning just seven points through their first 15 regular-season matches.

Since the World Cup break, this team have picked up 39 points in this competition, a big reason why they are in the playoffs for a 10th time.

While Ryan Richter’s time as interim boss has been mostly positive, his team have dropped off a little of late, currently on a four-match winless run in this competition.

Richter could suffer successive defeats at Subaru Park this weekend for the first time since taking charge, with his team failing to win their last two home league games.

A victory on Saturday would guarantee they finish the regular season with 50 points or more for the fifth time in the last six years.

At Subaru Park, the Union have collected at least a point in six straight meetings against Toronto, netting three or more goals on four of those occasions.

While they were in playoff contention much longer than usual, it will be another waitful next year for Toronto.

Their defeat last weekend means that they have now missed the playoffs on six consecutive occasions, though a point on Saturday would mark just the second time this decade that they reach 40 points at the end of a regular season.

Robin Fraser’s men could equal their longest losing run away from home this year (three) with a defeat on Saturday, losing three more MLS away matches in 2026 (10) than they did last year.

A win this weekend would give them four on the road this season, surpassing their total from 2025.

Their struggles away from home have been their undoing once again, with TFC conceding three or more goals in seven domestic encounters as the visitors this year.

Toronto are unbeaten in five of their last six regular season meetings with Philly, earning points in their last two visits to Subaru Park.

Philadelphia Union Major League Soccer form:

Due to a knee injury, Geiner Martinez is questionable for the Union on Saturday, as is Japhet Sery Larsen, who could sit out with a concussion, Milan Iloski has a hamstring strain and Alejandro Bedoya is doubtful because of an eye injury.

Danley Jean Jacques is eligible to return from his suspension, while Jovan Lukic and Jesus Bueno might both be rested, seeing as they are just a caution away from being suspended.

As for Toronto, Walker Zimmerman has an ankle strain, Nelson Palacio is doubtful due to a thigh injury, while Kobe Franklin has a sore shoulder and Alonso Coello will be suspended.

Lewis Morgan netted their only goal in the latter stages against the Five Stripes, while captain Jonathan Osorio became the first Reds player to reach 350 regular-season caps, with Richie Laryea getting to 150.

Philadelphia Union possible starting lineup:

Blake; Westfield, Pierre, Harriel, Wagner; Q. Sullivan, Vassilev, Jean Jacques, Bender; Alladoh, Damiani

Gavran; Laryea, Kuscevic, Gomis, Pereira; Osorio, Cimermancic, Mihailovic, Etienne; Sargent, Corbeanu

The Reds' defensive woes have hurt them all season, and against a Union side that have netted multiple goals in seven MLS home games since August, we believe Philly will overpower the Reds this weekend.

#2259. ‘Almost guaranteed’: Ex-Wrexham man drops Sheffield Wednesday promotion verdict

Sheffield Wednesday have turned to the Chester-born man as their new head coach as they aim for promotion from League One. SWFC have picked him as their replacement for Henrik Pedersen, who was sacked after back-to-back defeats against Notts County and Doncaster Rovers.

Challinor, 51, parted ways with Stockport County at the end of last season. He guided the Hatters from the National League to the third tier during his time at Edgeley Park.

Tozer, who played for the likes of Newport County and Cheltenham Town as well as Wrexham, has delivered his verdict on the Owls on the latest episode of The Essential EFL Podcast: “They’re nine points off top and they’ve got a game in hand. That quickly turns to six points and they’re looking at the top two positions, especially with the players they’ve got in there.

“It’s not a million miles away. I actually thought they were a little lower down the division then they actually are. Play-offs is an absolute minimum. I’d actually say that it’s almost guaranteed that they’ll get in the play-offs.”

Challinor is a promotion specialist in the lower leagues and worked wonders at Stockport County. He won 54% of his 268 games at the helm of the North West club.

He left the Hatters in June following their play-off final defeat to Bolton Wanderers at Wembley. The ex-AFC Fylde and Hartlepool United man needed a break and will be feeling refreshed now as he looks to sink his teeth into a new challenge at Hillsborough.

Sheffield Wednesday had a summer of transition with plenty of comings and goings. It has taken a while for their new boys to all gel together.

Their form hasn’t been great over recent weeks, hence why Pedersen was given the chop. Patience may need to be required whilst Challinor gets to grips with his new surroundings and looks to get his ideas across to the players.

Sheffield Wednesday’s first assignment under Challinor is an away trip to Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup this weekend. This Sunday’s clash is a chance for SWFC to start with a win and build some momentum.

The Owls then have an EFL Trophy tie against Accrington Stanley next Wednesday which is likely to be a match for some fringe players and youngsters to get minutes. They take on Plymouth Argyle on their own patch after the international break on Saturday 14th November.

Challinor will have plenty of time to work with the players on the training ground over the coming weeks. He will be under pressure immediately to get results as Sheffield Wednesday look to claw themselves back out of League One at the first time of asking.
