#2258. Ryan Jack knew Clement was doomed at Rangers as he reveals bad taste in his mouth

A Tuesday night in May and Rangers are hosting Dundee in their last home league game of the season. After seven years, one league title, a Scottish Cup medal and a European final, Ryan Jack is preparing to say farewell to Ibrox.

“I took along all my family that night thinking, ‘it might be my last home game,” he tells Herald Sport.

“I had been to the hotel all day with the team all days and had lunch and a sleep before the bus to Ibrox.

“We got to the stadium pre-match and nobody has said a word to me, so I am thinking I am in the squad and might play…”

Any realistic hope of Rangers winning the league title had extinguished the previous week after a defeat at Parkhead.

Fully recovered from a muscle injury, Jack still had plenty to play for. His contract was up at the end of the season and the Scottish Cup Final against Celtic was just around the corner. Despite injuries corrupting his season he felt sure he still had something to offer.

He had no idea if Philippe Clement felt the same. Whenever he knocked on the manager’s door pinning the Belgian down was like nailing jelly to the wall.

When his name was absent from the squad list for Dundee, he finally had his answer. At 32, he had been around football long enough to sense which way the wind was blowing.

“That was after seven years of graft and putting my life into every day and playing through pain barriers, because that’s what you do.

“That night against Dundee was when I thought that it was not going to work out for Clement at Rangers.

“Because when other people see you treat players who had been there for seven years like that then they say, ‘nah, he’s miles off it.’

“Players are human and it’s like any walk of life. If your boss does that you think, ‘nah, that’s not on.’

Currently a free agent after two years in Turkey, the former Scotland midfielder is taking his time over what comes next. At 34 he wants to play on after the kind of family Christmas he never managed when the festive fixture card piled up one game after another at Aberdeen and Rangers. When he sits down to write some cards to family and friends its safe to assume that the name of the current Norwich City manager won’t be on the list.

“I actually remember guys like Jack Butland, Conor Goldson and James Tavernier and John Lundstram saying to me, ‘that’s an absolute disgrace what the manager has done to you.’

“When that happened the penny dropped that I was definitely going to be leaving for sure.”

For long periods of season 2023/24 Rangers had looked a reasonable bet for the title. Four points adrift of Brendan Rodgers’ Celtic the Ibrox side had the saving grace of two games in hand until they threw the advantage away. They lost the first game in Dingwall to Ross County then failed to find a way through against Dundee in a goalless draw at Dens Park.

By the time they turned around a two goal deficit against the Dark Blues in their final Ibrox home game it was too little, too late. Celtic rattled five goals past Kilmarnock the following night and won their 12th championship in 13 seasons and faith in Clement was draining fast.

An unused substitute as Celtic won the Scottish Cup final with a late Adam Idah goal, Jack delayed his holiday plans to hang back in Glasgow and prepare for a trip to the Euros with Scotland. Despite asking the question - repeatedly - he still had no idea what the future held.

“There were talks ongoing for months and months. It looked like a deal might be happening, then it wasn’t happening.

“You know what it’s like. Managers start having a certain way of communicating with you.

“I went to see him two or three times to say, ‘okay what’s happening here?’

“I wanted to plan for the future and every time it was, ‘oh, we don’t know yet, the club has not made a decision. It’s not my decision.’

“All I wanted was an honest answer. I felt that, when I was fit and well I was still capable of doing a job for sure.

“There were just certain things that made me think my time was coming to an end.”.

As it turned out his last appearance for Rangers came in a 2-2 draw against Benfica in the last 16 of the Europa League in Lisbon in March. A late replacement for Tom Lawrence in the Stadium of Light, a muscle injury ruled him out until he returned to fitness at the end of the season. Even then he didn’t play.

His surprise inclusion in Steve Clarke’s Scotland squad for the Euros in Germany was a reward for his dogged loyalty and performances at international level. He felt none of that payback coming from Philippe Clement.

“I was training for a couple of weeks after the season finished because I wanted to keep ticking over for Euros.

“It was then that he (Clement) phoned me and asked for 10 minutes to speak to me.

“He came in to the training centre and he said that the club had made a decision to go younger and I had suffered a few injuries over my time and that hadn’t helped me with the people above who had doubts.

“He said they wanted to go down a young route and get most of the experienced players out.

“And my comeback to him was, ‘do you think that is the best way to go?’

“A club like Rangers can not just sign a team of players who are 20 or 21 or 22 and expect them to be ready for the expectation and the pressure and the European stage.

“I’ve always felt that, to be successful at Rangers, you need a core who can be around the changing room and lead it. Even if they don’t play every week.

“But he wanted to go young and it didn’t turn out too well for him.”

“That time under Clement left a bad taste - the way he was going about things at the end.”

Jack had known nothing beyond the narrow horizons of Scottish football. Joining Aberdeen’s pro youth system at the age of eight, he rose to become captain of his hometown club. When he took advantage of freedom of contract to sign a three year deal with Rangers in 2017 some took his choice of club badly. His parents’ home address in Aberdeen was posted online accompanied by brainless threats.

“You know what? I grew up a Rangers fan as a young boy and the move wasn’t difficult for me personally.

“I know that the clubs are huge rivals and people take that personally, but I still hold Aberdeen in the highest regard. There was no ill feeling towards the club at all.

“Making the move was always a football decision to go to a new level and play in front of 50,000 people in Europe with a better training facility at the time.

“Off the pitch it brought a lot of stuff and hype media wise, of course it did.

“I’m not going to lie, I felt that pressure when I went back to Pittodrie. Everyone is against you.

“My family stay in the north east and they probably got a bit more of that than I did.

“There were maybe people at Aberdeen who looked at me differently for moving to Rangers, I don’t know.”

One of those who took news of the move to Ibrox badly, ironically, was his Aberdeen manager at the time. One Derek McInnes esq.

“Derek told me that after we played Rangers Pedro Caixinha was in giving it, ‘were going to take your players.’

“Derek had a wee chat with me asking what I was planning. He gave me the captaincy at the club and wanted to build the team around me.

“I loved my time with Derek. Working with him was one of the most enjoyable periods of my career.

“We had a wee bit of a fall out towards the end of the season because he was desperate for me to stay with Aberdeen.

“I wanted to move on and develop my career away from the club, but we are brand new now.”

There was a time, shortly after his own arrival at Ibrox in 2017, when it seemed that McInnes would join him in Glasgow. Jack was told by former Aberdeen teammates that the deal was done until a late u-turn.

McInnes stayed put at Pittodrie, only leaving when he was dismissed in 2021. He found a route to Ibrox eventually - via Kilmarnock and Hearts - while the club which fired him goes through managers like Donald Trump sweats his way through MAGA caps.

Involved with Aberdeen for 17 years Jack thinks the decision to part company with McInnes was a mistake.

“Derek was always made for being at a big club and having that demand on him.

“He knows the league and he knows how to get the job done. And when I look at Aberdeen they have never been able to replace him.

“I think back to the team he assembled when I was there, with Kenny McLean, Adam Rooney, Graeme Shinnie, Jonny Hayes, Niall McGinn, Mark Reynolds, Andy Considine, Shay Logan. Even James Maddison came in on loan for a time.

“We had great characters and we were always finishing second in the league and getting to semi finals and finals every year and yet being captain the whole vibe outside was, ‘we’re not good enough, we cannot compete.’ It was always negative.

“But we were up against Brendan Rodgers and a strong Celtic squad.

“And I don’t think that Aberdeen team that we had will be matched for years to come.

“I don’t know what direction the club wants to go in now or what kind of recruitment they go for.

“It’s mix and match, here, there and everywhere. Managers go from Stephen Glass to Jim Goodwin and Neil Warnock to Stephen Robinson. Yet when I look back at my time with Del it was so settled.”

After two seasons with Turkish club Esenler Erokspor Jack is enjoying some down time back in Scotland. His relationship with McInnes is back on a firm footing and he uses the gym facilities at Auchenhowie to keep fit while he waits for the right offer to get back playing. He is open minded on what comes next.

“I want to play on. I am only 34 and I still feel that I have a bit to give.

“There is no real plan, but I have had a few discussions here and there at a decent enough level with some teams. I want to wait and see what comes up, it needs to be the right place at the right time.

“I am not arrogant about where I play or what I want to do, I have had a good career so far. But I want something that excites me and gives me something to look forward to.”

When the news broke that Jack was using Rangers facilities to keep fit, a post from his former manager Steven Gerrard in Instagram almost broke the internet.

“Put him in the middle,” wrote the England legend. “Proper player.”

“I think that ship has sailed,” says Jack, with a laugh. “I loved my time at the club and have some amazing memories.

“I think Stevie had just seen that I had been training in there and it was a tongue in cheek comment.

“I have said before that the time under Stevie Gerrard was the best football I played in my career.

“Everything he brought when he came in opened my eyes to a new world.”

By  his own admission, he made the most of the talent he had. If anything there were times, under Gerrard, when he pushed his body farther than harder than he should have and it hurt him.

“If I could do more I would do it. If I could train harder I would train harder. If I could run more in a game I did it.

“That’s what the gaffer loved about me. I would never say no.

“I would never say, ‘I’m not training’ or ‘i’m not playing.’

“And that maybe cost me a few injuries over the years.”

Clubs looking for an old head with a ferocious work rate should know that he now feels fit and ready to go. He wants to play while preparing for the next phase.

“It has always fascinated me, managing and having your own team. I want to go down and avenue and see where that leads me…”

#2257. GdS: From a curse to a blessing – the factors behind Milan’s recent derby dominance

AC Milan won the derby again on Saturday night, continuing their recent dominance of the fixture, despite Inter celebrating more silverware.

La Gazzetta dello Sport write that Milan planted their flag in the middle of the Duomo and have not removed it for two years. Something strange is happening in the city, frankly inexplicable: Inter haven’t won in eight derbies, even though on paper they’re almost always the stronger team.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Since September 2024, the Nerazzurri have had eight matches against Milan, losing six and drawing too. But why does this keep happening? The paper have attempted to offer some explanations.

Between January 2023 and April 2024, Inter won six consecutive derbies. It became Stefano Pioli’s curse. Three matches were more difficult than the others: the two 2023 Champions League semi-finals and the April 2024 derby, in which Simone Inzaghi’s side sealed the Scudetto.

It was the pinnacle for Inter fans and the worst nightmare for Milan fans, not coincidentally commemorated last night by the Nord with its choreography. From that moment on, Milan turned things around.

In September 2024, they won with Matteo Gabbia’s 89th-minute header, and it all changed from there. A pair of draws, then the 3-0 Coppa Italia win with a Luka Jovic brace, the two 1-0 wins last season (Pulisic in the first leg, Estupinan in the return), and this 3-1 win most recently.

For the reasons, you should consult a psychologist, but some things can be understood. Milan certainly play much more freely than during those terrible 15 months.

They’re definitely finding tactical solutions they didn’t have in that run under Pioli, where they pressed hard and ended up only allowing counter-attacks. They especially struggled greatly against Dimarco, Calhanoglu, and Mkhitaryan.

Paulo Fonseca initiated the change, reinforced by Sergio Conceiçao in the Supercoppa Italiana final, by Massimiliano Allegri, and now by Amorim. Milan are increasingly confident and, as much as possible in a crazy situation like the derby, control the games.

This time, they struggled very little, even in the second half, as if they had the password to counter Inter’s moves. Much of it was a question of energy: Milan no longer fear their cousins, they go head-to-head, and often win the duels in recent derbies.

Then, of course, there are the individuals. Christian Pulisic is becoming a ghost for Inter, fitting of these times. CP11 has scored in four of the last eight derbies, and they’ve all been crucial goals. With his intelligence, he understands where to strike Inter. With his speed, he surprises them.

As for that intelligence thing, Luka Modric is second to none. In the derby that Pervis Estupinan won, he was the best on the field. In Saturday’s resounding win, he helped the team manage the tempo, defended up front, winning back valuable balls, and even left the free kick to Moreira.

Add Adrien Rabiot to the list of Inter nightmares: ‘the Duke’ has earned 7.5, 7, and 7.5 again in the three Milan derbies he has played (Gazzetta ratings). Throwing all that together, and you get a one-sided fixture.

I think the broadcast showed stats that said out of the 29 matches where Pulisic has scored Milan had lost only three and won the rest. So basically Milan will win if he scores.

Two teams with only pride to play for on MLS Decision Day will meet on Saturday as the New York Red Bulls close out the regular season against Atlanta United at Sports Illustrated Stadium.

A 3-2 defeat at Nashville SC officially eliminated the Red Bulls from playoff contention, while the Five Stripes beat Toronto 3-1 but sit 13th in the East, three places below their upcoming opponents.

Before the start of 2025, the Red Bulls had the longest run of playoff appearances in the league (15 years), but now their fans will not see any post-season fixtures for their club in back-to-back campaigns.

Michael Bradley failed to get them over the playoff line this time around, and their defeat last weekend means they can finish with 42 points at most this season, which would be their worst-ever 34-match campaign regarding points.

They cooled off at the most inopportune moment in 2026, losing four of their last five domestic affairs heading into this game.

Defensively, they have a lot of work to do in the off-season, boasting the second-highest expected goals conceded average in the Eastern Conference this year (65.3).

In the 2026 regular season, this team have dropped 10 points when scoring first in MLS, though only two of them occurred at Sports Illustrated Stadium.

The Red Bulls are undefeated at home versus Atlanta, winning this exact fixture last year by a 2-0 score.

For the first time in club history, Atlanta United have failed to make the playoffs on consecutive occasions, lacking consistency all season long.

Gerardo Martino’s side will finish slightly better than last season regarding points, boasting 33 so far, five more than they managed in what was their worst-ever 34-game campaign in 2025.

That said, if they suffer a worse result than DC United on Decision Day, Atlanta would end 2026 in 14th place, matching their worst-ever placement in the Eastern Conference from last season.

One minor positive is that if they win this weekend, they will reach five road victories for the year, marking their best total this decade.

Consistency in front of goal has hurt them in 2026, with the Five Stripes averaging the third-fewest goals per game among Eastern Conference teams (1.3).

Atlanta United have points in their last three Decision Day outings, all outside Mercedes-Benz Stadium, including a 2-1 win at Orlando City in their final regular-season outing of 2024.

#2255. Derby County vs Bolton Wanderers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 04 Nov 2026 23:07, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:09

Struggling Derby County will look to claim their first win since the start of September when they play host to Bolton Wanderers on Friday.

The Rams are languishing in the relegation zone, while the Trotters are sitting in 17th spot in the Championship table.

After finishing in eighth spot last term, Derby came into this campaign with hopes of mounting a serious playoff bid in their third consecutive season in the second tier.

However, those aspirations have been quickly replaced by relegation fears after winning just one of their 14 league matches this season (D4, L9).

The Rams have drawn three and lost seven of their 10 matches since claiming their solitary victory in an away clash at Portsmouth on September 1, leaving them bottom but one in the rankings.

In fact, they have lost four of their last five games, including heavy defeats in their last two outings against Millwall and Norwich City.

After losing 4-1 away to the Lions, Derby conceded four more goals without reply in their midweek home clash against Norwich, extending their wait for their first home win of the season while leaving them with the second-worst defensive record in the division.

With Derby also the joint-lowest scorers in the Championship, under-pressure John Eustace will be searching for inspiration ahead of the club's final outing before the international break, and he may find it in the fact the Rams have won each of their last six home games against Bolton.

Bolton, meanwhile, have established a seven-point gap to the drop zone in their first Championship campaign since 2018-19.

The Trotters will head into Friday's contest in a positive mood after ending a three-game winless run with a narrow 1-0 victory over Charlton Athletic on Tuesday, which came courtesy of Sam Dalby's sixth goal of the season.

That result made it back-to-back away clean sheets and extended their unbeaten run on the road to four matches (W3, D1).

That is a significant improvement on the early stages of the season, which saw them take just one point from their opening three road trips in the Championship.

In regard to their head-to-head record, they have not beaten Derby in their last 10 away encounters since claiming a 2-1 win in the EFL Cup in October 1999 (D3, L6).

In fact, Bolton, who have never won a league game at Pride Park, are looking to record their first head-to-head away win over Derby in the EFL since picking up a 2-1 victory at the Baseball Ground in September 1981.

Derby are continuing to deal with a considerable absentee list that features Eiran Cashin, Sondre Langas, Craig Forsyth, Derry Murkin and Charlie Taylor.

Matt Clarke and Patrick Agyemang have returned to full training, but it is unclear whether the important duo will be ready for Friday’s fixture.

There are also doubts surrounding the fitness of Joe Ward, Ryan Hedges, David Ozoh and Sammie Szmodics, the latter of whom was forced off in Tuesday’s defeat to Norwich.

As for the visitors, Gavin Bazunu, Eoin Toal, Ethan Erhahon and Ryan Hardie are all unlikely to feature after missing the last two matches due to injury.

Steven Schumacher is also expected to be without the attacking duo of John McAtee and Jayden Nelson due to knee and hamstring injuries respectively.

There are also concerns over Josh Sheehan and Atsuki Ito, who both appeared to pick up injuries in Tuesday’s victory over Charlton.

Widell Zetterstrom; Johnston, Nyambe, Sanderson, Robinson; Travis, Clark; Fuseini, Bilbija, Brewster; Morris

Bolton Wanderers possible starting lineup:

Bonham; Larrazabal, Forino-Joseph, Famewo; Conway; Simons, Watson; Iling-Junior, Rodrigues, Gale; Dalby

With Derby bereft of confidence, Friday's fixture represents a great opportunity for Bolton to end their 45-year wait for an away league win over the Rams.

We believe the Trotters will rise to the challenge, especially as they have enjoyed recent success on the road with three victories in their last four away games.

#2254. Chris Wilder must reintroduce Sheffield United award-winner vs Charlton Athletic after new injury update: Opinion

Sheffield United were victorious in the week as they defeated Preston North End 3-2. It was Sydie Peck who stole the headlines with his first-half hat-trick, and he was partnered in midfield by Jairo Riedewald in the absence of SUFC’s reigning player of the month, Kalvin Phillips.

Riedewald has had a tough season to date, but he acted as the deeper-sitting man in the middle, keeping the tempo and guarding the backline while Peck was allowed to push on and break into goalscoring positions. It was a steady display from the Dutchman, who had not started a Championship game since September 5th.

However, ahead of the upcoming clash with Charlton Athletic, Chris Wilder has confirmed that Phillips has been passed fit and will travel with the squad down to London. If he is deemed ready to do so, he must be put straight back into the Blades’ XI.

After injury prematurely ended his first Sheffield United loan stay, Phillips has found an influential role over the past two months. The former Leeds United star is usually the deepest-sitting of the starting midfielders, guarding the backline and looking for long balls into the channels.

His 9.58 defensive contributions per 90 ranks him in the top 3% of Championship midfielders (FotMob), and he stands out in duels too. Phillips averages 7.84 duels won (top 9%) and 2.32 aerial duels won (top 5%) per match, and his passing range is shown by 3.63 accurate long balls (top 9%) too.

Phillips acts as the shield for the backline while his midfield partner is usually given freedom to venture forward and be more expressive. Peck has been operating in this role for the most part, and he was at his best in the midweek triumph over Preston North End.

Wilder has been using more of a 4-4-2 setup in recent weeks, and Phillips and Peck make for an excellently balanced partnership. It is one that could bring SUFC real success over the course of this season.

Phillips’ injury problems are well known, so it could be that Sheffield United lean on the side of caution and opt against throwing him straight back into the XI against Charlton Athletic.

If that is the case, Riedewald should keep his spot. He is similar to Phillips in his defensive play style, although he perhaps doesn’t boast the same technical ability or passing range.

Peck’s success at Deepdale showed Riedewald is a good facilitator though, and Wilder should not hesitate to call on him again.

Hamza Choudhury is an alternative option, but he has been needed at right-back with Femi Seriki expected to work his way back to full fitness after the international break.

#2253. Martin O'Neill issues fresh Joel van den Berg verdict after Celtic B appearance

Martin O'Neill says he'll demand 'a lot more' of Joel van den Berg after watching the midfielder in action for Celtic B against Ross County on Tuesday evening.

O'Neill travelled to Dingwall with coaches Mark Fotheringham and Stephen McManus for the Scottish Challenge Cup tie.

Along with Van den Berg, first-team duo Shim Mheuka and Landon Emenalo featured from the start as Celtic B lost 2-0.

The Netherlands youth international, signed from PSV Eindhoven for £3.5 million during the summer transfer window, completed 72 minutes.

"He's just back from injury. I thought his training sessions had been very, very good," O'Neill said of Van den Berg. "And I'm going to demand a lot more of him."

O'Neill hasn't shied away from giving youth a chance since returning as Celtic manager last year.

Colby Donovan has emerged as a first-team regular during his second and third tenures, with numerous youngsters involved in pre-season a few months back.

The 74-year-old even spoke to the players after Tuesday's defeat.

"That was wildly important for me to go up to that game [in Dingwall], really," O'Neill added. "Because there are two things to it.

"Young boys want to see the manager there. And I do go. I went to a lot of the B team games last year as well down at Barrowfield. I feel that that's my job at the end of the day.

"And while I might not be around, seriously, to see the likes of Liam Kennedy or people like this to mature and become [first-team players], you would take at least half an ounce of satisfaction that you saw them and that you were at least saying something to them that might help them get out of it.

"The new manager coming in might just give totally different advice. I don't know. But I think that my advice to young players, this is not boastful, I promise you, I think it's the right way.

"I give you an instance which I said to the team the other night. They were beaten by a very, very strong, and committed Ross County team.

"But it was important for me to see, first of all, the young Chelsea lads playing, then Joel [van den Berg] coming back from injury, seeing what he's capable of doing.

"But my things will not change in this game. Whatever changes they make to the game, you have the defenders to be able to defend. You've got full-backs whose first job is to think about stopping crosses from coming in. And then when they want to come and join in and be something.

"You need your attacking midfield players on the half-turn, making something happen. Don't be playing it back all day long. I could do that.

"You need your centre forwards to get hold of the ball – that's something that Brian Clough preached. He didn’t care whether the centre forwards scored the goals, as long as they held the ball up and played people into the game.

"You also need wingers to take people on. Those things do not change. And it doesn't matter about tactics. It doesn't matter about jumping. It doesn't matter about man for man. It doesn't matter about all of these things. It's what you do.

"I've seen it a thousand times, players with terrific ability when they're 17, 18, being given the wrong information and not realising their potential because they didn't have it."

#2250. Scotland boss hails 'buy-in' as senior trio embrace Under-21 drop

Scotland Under-21s head coach Tony Docherty is delighted with the buy-in from the three players who have dropped down from the senior squad.

Colby Donovan, Lennon Miller and James Wilson have all been included in a new-look Scotland Under-21 squad for friendlies against Ukraine and United States in Spain this month.

The three players were all in Sebastian Pocogonoli’s first full Scotland squad and join Tyler Fletcher, who went to the World Cup before joining Docherty’s group in September.

“I’m really, really delighted with the introduction of the ones that have had that A squad experience, and they’re really the drivers,” Docherty said. “They become your leadership group that drives standards, create that mentality, get that identity.

“You’ve seen that in the last camp when I was out at the A squad camp and I returned with James and Findlay (Curtis), and then Findlay goes back up again as a result of his performance.

“Lennon and Colby Donovan, I’ve had dialogue with both of them quite a bit over the last couple of days, just to outline what their pathway is.

“If they’re not going to be getting minutes for this camp with the A squad, it’s important they come in to my squad and it will be a mirror image in terms of structure, fundamentals. And they just come in and get an opportunity to get game time.

“So, really pleased that Lennon, Colby and you can include Fletch, James in that, they don’t just come to the 21s, they really participate.

“I had a particularly long conversation with Lennon. You’ve seen the example, the last camp there with Barney Stewart. He comes in, he scores three goals, he’s in a good place when he gets back, gets into West Brom’s first team, scores a couple of goals, they win a couple of games.

“I had a good conversation with Colby and he just wants to play. So it’s important that we’ve got the buy-in. It’s not us telling Colby, ‘you’re playing with the 21s’, it’s him in dialogue with us saying, ‘I want to do that, I want to play and I want to impress’.

“And that’s very much where we are now, and that’s a relationship I think is really healthy.”

St Mirren striker Eseosa Sule has been called up as Docherty prepares for a new European qualifying campaign after 16 of the 27 players from his first squad went beyond the age level.

Rangers youth player Kyle Glasgow drops to the Scotland Under-19 squad for three European qualifying games. The 16-year-old on-loan Alloa player joins elder brother Euan Glasgow, who plays for Edinburgh City on loan from Hearts.

#2247. Former Wolves, Sheffield United and Leicester City man retires aged 33 after ‘tough’ recent years

Conor Coady was let go by Wrexham in August, with the club and player agreeing a mutual termination of his contract. The former Wolves captain has been without a club since, although there were links with a move to Swansea City shortly before his departure from the Racecourse Ground.

Now though, aged 33, Coady has announced his decision to retire from professional football. The defender confirmed the news on his Instagram, with a video thanking his former clubs, coaches, teammates, family and friends for their support along the way.

Coady hangs up his boots after over 500 club career appearances and 10 England caps. He played 317 times for Wolverhampton Wanderers and also spent time with Sheffield United, Huddersfield Town, Leicester City, Everton, Charlton Athletic, Wrexham and Liverpool.

In a video message shared on his Instagram, Coady bid a fond farewell to the game. He said:

“Football is my life. It always has been, but everything at some point comes to an end. I never ever thought that playing for Wolves in Europe or going to with England a World Cup this day would come. But honestly, the last couple of years have been tough – not playing as much as I would have liked I have struggled with, as I have lived for playing footy.

“Coming through at my boyhood club and putting the red shirt on was one of the proudest days, as no one could ever take that away from me. Sheffield United and Huddersfield were the perfect clubs for me and made me grow, then came Wolves, the place I needed and the place that made me a man.

“Eight of the best years, with the best people, in a city that made me their own. My kids were born in Old Gold, and you made Amie (Coady’s wife) truly enjoy football. For that, it will always be home.”

Coady would go on to highlight his pride at having played for Everton at Goodison Park, before reflecting on tougher times at Leicester City and Wrexham. He said:

“On to Leicester, then Wrexham, which were tough at times but filled with amazing people and amazing supporters who care deeply about their club. The Charlton, who put a smile back on my face.

“The truth is, I’m ready now. I’ve given football everything and I will continue to do so in my next chapter, but it’s time for my incredible wife Amie and my four boys to enjoy seeing Daddy that little bit more.”

After personal thanks to his close family, friends, supporters and long-serving agent James Lippett, Coady signed off by saying: “It’s been an honour to say I played this unbelievable game. Thank you for everything. Coads.”

Coady has cut a popular figure among fans and teammates alike across his playing career. It means there was no shortage of support for the retiring defender upon his announcement.

Wolves players of past and present sent their best wishes, including Kieran Trippier, Raul Jimenez, Romain Saiss, Daniel Podence and many more. Ex-Everton teammates Jack Grealish and James Tarkowski congratulated him too, alongside other prominent figures like former England boss Gareth Southgate, retired boxer Tony Bellew and broadcasters Kelly Somers and Suzi Perry.

Coady has worked in punditry in recent years, so it will be intriguing to see if he ventures further into this world after his retirement. If a future in coaching awaits, his personable character and leadership abilities would make him an excellent addition to a club’s academy or first-team staff.

#2246. Liverpool transfer news: Andoni Iraola could lose £15m attacker to Premier League 'admirers' after disastrous spell

By Lewis Nolan | 04 Nov 2026 23:24

Liverpool attacker Harvey Elliot could sign for Leeds United permanently in the January transfer window, the latest report has claimed.

The Reds attacker is currently on loan at Valencia, though he has not enjoyed a particularly productive spell in Spain, with the 23-year-old yet to start for the club this season.

Elliott was in fact sent off against Villarreal on October 24, and rumours have suggested that Valencia could end his loan this winter.

Liverpool boss Andoni Iraola did not show that he trusted the Englishman during pre-season, and with his contract set to expire in summer 2028, a permanent exit may make sense.

Football Insider claim that Leeds United would be prepared to move for him in the January transfer window if an opportunity arose.

Elliott is valued by Transfermarkt at just £15m, but it would be surprising if the Reds managed to recoup anywhere close to that fee in the winter.

However, the attacker is unlikely to earn any minutes for Iraola, whose system requires a level of intensity that he has not yet shown he is capable of, and sitting on the bench would only further deteriorate his value.

While a new loan should not be ruled out, it remains to be seen if Elliott would be open to another temporary exit given his move to Aston Villa last season was also disastrous.

If Leeds show any interest in the 23-year-old this January, the Reds should facilitate a sale, even if the fee they receive is relatively modest.

One of the reasons for Elliott's struggles is that he has been largely overlooked since 2024, with managers like Unai Emery and Arne Slot not even using him as a rotation option.

The 23-year-old played just 371 minutes of Premier League football in 2024-25, and he played 113 minutes of league football last season.

While Elliott must bear responsibility for some of the fitness issues that have been raised during his time in Spain, it is understandable that he is not at his best considering his turbulent last two years.

#2242. 6 Michael Skubala replacements Bristol City must eye as sack calls grow

Bristol City suffered a 2-1 loss to Wolves on Wednesday night to fall to 15th in the Championship table.

The Robins appointed Michael Skubala as head coach in the summer, but the former Lincoln City boss is already facing scrutiny surrounding his position.

The 44-year-old has overseen just five wins from 14 league games so far this campaign, with the team earning just one victory from their last six fixtures.

This fan thinks that defeat against Norwich City over the weekend could spell the end.

@TWMCLtd, wrote: "We have a tremendous squad. Injuries improving. But Skubala has not got a Skubee-Doo. Still refusing to play Horvat. Non sensical substitutions. Getting toxic now. Sadly think he needs to go."

@_W00KS_ and @Mendip_muddler were locked in debate.

​​​​​​​@lpmoffatt says he's running out of patience with Skubala: "The lack of any identity or nouse is the biggest concern. It it were long ball and Tolaj was real useful in the air with players buzzing around him we might stomach it but just aimlessly trying to hit him whilst sitting deep isn't working. No plan B. Absolutely no patience left."

Bristol City initially took the lead against Wolves through Scott Twine in the 34th minute, but a late brace from Sean Neave sealed all three points for the Old Gold at Ashton Gate.

This has put Skubala under immense pressure just a few days before the November international break, which could be an opportunity for clubs to make a change in the dugout.

Here we look at six ambitious replacements the Championship side must consider as sack calls grow…

Rob Edwards is the most obvious immediately available candidate given his previous work at both Luton Town and Middlesbrough.

He guided the Hatters to promotion to the Premier League in 2023, and had Boro in the mix for a top two spot before jumping ship to Wolves midway through last season.

HIs impressive track record speaks for itself, and if he can be convinced to make the move to Ashton Gate, then he could be a very strong appointment.

There will be competition for his appointment, so Bristol City may need to act quickly in order to secure his signature.

Does Michael Skubala deserve more time to turn things around at Bristol City? Comment your opinion below

Steven Gerrard is currently available after leaving his role with Al-Ettifaq in January 2025, and he could be looking to make his return to management in English football with a move to the Championship.

The former midfielder enjoyed tremendous success in his first role as a head coach at Rangers, leading the Glasgow outfit to a league title in 2021, as well as a Europa League final a year later.

Neto Borges could have been at Bristol City, but Kim Hellberg's reintegrated him into the Boro side, while the Robins loaned in a good replacement

His time in Scotland was defined by a strong defensive core that helped make his Rangers tough to beat, and it translated well on the continent, earning the club a first European final since 2008.

While his time at Aston Villa didn't go as smoothly, he could still be capable of replicating his Rangers success at Ashton Gate, provided he can build a strong coaching team around him.

Robbie Keane has yet to make his first foray into management in English football, although he did previously work as a coach at Middlesbrough and Leeds.

But he has carved out experience in Israel and Hungary, leading Maccabi Tel Aviv to a league title in 2024, and Ferencvaros to the 2025 title.

The former Ireland international also won the Magyar Kupa in 2026, before leaving the European side at the end of that campaign, which could now open the door for him to make the move to Bristol City.

It would certainly be a risk to bring him in given his lack of experience at this level, but his success abroad offers a glimpse of what he could achieve for the Robins.

Carlos Corberan was dismissed by Valencia in September after an underwhelming start to the new La Liga campaign.

But he has an impressive track record at Championship level, leading Huddersfield Town to a play-off final in 2023, and his West Brom side were also in the mix for a top six finish before he made the switch to Spain.

The 43-year-old is a smart tactician with an attractive style of play, which could make him quite an appealing appointment, particularly as the current squad would be suited to his ideal 4-2-3-1 formation.

His two previous Championship roles had the respective sides competing for promotion, and that is what Bristol City should be looking for when searching for any new head coaches.

The fear, here, might be Corberan eyeing a role in the Premier League.

Steve Cooper led Nottingham Forest to promotion and then survival in the Premier League, playing a pragmatic but effective style of play.

While his time at Leicester City didn’t prove as successful, events at the King Power Stadium since then indicate that there were bigger concerns going on than his coaching.

The 46-year-old could be a shrewd appointment by the Robins to get the team back into promotion contention, if he can be convinced to join a Championship side.

While his style of play has its detractors, there is no denying that it can get results when everything clicks.

#2241. ‘I’d back them’: Ex-EFL man shares his promotion favourites out of Huddersfield Town, Stockport County and Oxford United

Huddersfield Town are top of the table after 14 fixtures and have lost only once in the league this season. HTAFC are three points clear of Stockport County and Oxford United, with Luton Town, Plymouth Argyle and Reading then chasing them down.

The Terriers drew 1-1 at home to Luton Town last time out with in-form attacker Joe Taylor getting their goal against the Hatters. They are back in action this weekend against Exeter City in the first round of the FA Cup.

Tozer, who played for Newport County, Cheltenham Town and Wrexham, has delivered his verdict on the third tier promotion picture on The Essential EFL Podcast: “Take nothing away from Stockport, Luton, Oxford, Plymouth Argyle. They are big clubs in there looking to get out of that division. It is so tight as well.

“When you look at the top of that division, Stockport are playing superb football. They’re playing really nice stuff, easy on the eye but Huddersfield have that pedigree behind them. They are, in my eyes, a Championship club. I’m pretty confident to say come the end of the season, I’d back them to be in that top two.”

Huddersfield Town’s decision to appoint Martin Drury as their head coach in May has worked out very well for them so far. He has worked wonders in the HTAFC dugout in this campaign and has won 58% of games since he was handed the full-time gig.

Drury, 40, joined the Terriers last January and worked under Liam Manning before taking over as caretaker in March. He has also worked as a coach in the past at Manchester United, West Brom, Valencia and Brentford so has an impressive CV behind him.

The West Yorkshire outfit have been a tough nut to crack in defence over recent times. They have let in only 12 goals this term and only Oxford United and Bradford City have a better defensive record.

At the other end of the pitch, Huddersfield Town have scored goals for fun and are very dangerous when they attack. They have found the net on 28 occasions which is the best in the division.

Huddersfield Town landed Derensili Sanches Fernandes in July from Excelsior on a four-year deal and he has since adapted with ease to life in England. He has become one of HTAFC’s main players and has made 15 outings, chipping in with an eye-catching seven goals and six assists.

Drury has also managed to get the best out of Taylor following his return from his loan at Wigan Athletic. He has found the net on 10 occasions in the league already.

The Terriers need to hope both Fernandes and Taylor can stay fit as they aim to keep their momentum going. Their upcoming cup tie against Exeter City could be an opportunity to play some fringe players and youngsters.

#2240. Man United transfer news: Scouts 'intensify' efforts to sign £38m defender as Michael Carrick endures crisis in the backline

By Lewis Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 00:27

Manchester United are in the race for Como defender Jacobo Ramon Naveros, the newest report has revealed.

It is understandable that the Red Devils are already looking forward to the next few transfer windows considering the club are 17th in the Premier League table with just seven points.

Michael Carrick's side are only above the relegation zone because of goal difference, and part of the reason they are so low is because of their defence.

United have conceded at least two goals five times in the Premier League so far this season, and the squad are in desperate need of reinforcements at the back.

A new report from TEAMtalk claims that United's scouting of Como defender Ramon is set to intensify, especially following his game against the Red Devils in the Champions League in October.

Ramon has primarily operated as a centre-back during his career, and the defender has shown that he can contribute at both ends of the pitch.

The 21-year-old managed to score against United after he rotated into midfield and decided to make an unchallenged run into the penalty area, before heading home from close range.

There are no question marks about the Spaniard's ability on the ball, but he also routinely wins his individual battles at the other end of the pitch, with the centre-back coming out on top in 67% of his duels in Serie A this season.

Though Ramon is valued by Transfermarkt at €45m (£37.51m), United will almost certainly have to pay significantly more than that price, but his potential is high.

It may sound hyperbolic, but United have one of the Premier League's weakest defences, with the likes of Luke Shaw and Harry Maguire far beyond their best.

Lisandro Martinez's mentality has been questioned, while many fans are keen for right-back Diogo Dalot to be replaced.

The need to strengthen at the back is urgent, and given United arguably find themselves in a relegation battle, they cannot afford to ignore their defence any longer.

#2238. Fan notices something remarkable about Scott Parker’s first 24 hours at West Ham

West Ham United began the Scott Parker era with a dramatic 3-3 draw against Cardiff City in South Wales on Wednesday night, and much has already been made of the 46-year-old's first days in charge.

After successive home defeats, the hierarchy at the London Stadium opted to fire Nuno Espírito Santo, before Parker was swiftly drafted in as the Portuguese's replacement, 15 years after controversially departing the Hammers for Tottenham Hotspur as a player.

The three-time Championship promotion winner will have already learned plenty about his new chargers at the Cardiff City Stadium, as they found themselves staring at a third straight loss through Jack Moylan's hat-trick.

However, the Irons were able to rally themselves to a point through strikes from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman.

It has been well-documented that Parker's team talk inside the dressing room was the catalyst for such a dramatic on-pitch transformation, but that wasn't the only talking point.

The former England international has previously gained notoriety for his fashion sense, and that came to the fore once again on social media.

Given the nature of Parker's appointment, the new West Ham boss has firmly been in the spotlight this week.

This included interviews with the club's in-house media team as well as the national press as he set his stall out for the future, but some eagle-eyed supporters have noticed a remarkable pattern in the process.

Indeed, it was noted by online personality, Huey Taylor, on Instagram, that Parker had been spotted wearing three different jackets in the space of '21 hours', including images of him applauding the travelling fans on Wednesday evening.

When explaining his observations, Taylor commented: "I'm genuinely convinced this is some sort of paid advertisement.

Referring back to some of Parker's previous clothing choices on the touchline at Fulham, AFC Bournemouth and Burnley, he added: "Those three stripes still remain.

He then joked: "It's like if Adidas did jackets for people from London with money."

Parker's favoured fashion brand on the touchline is that of Thom Browne, who founded his apparel business in New York City 25 years ago.

During his spell at Bournemouth, it was noticed that Parker sported a cardigan produced by the brand, which reportedly cost £1,350. It was then claimed, over the course of a 90-minute game, the cost-per-minute ratio worked out at £4.50.

Ironically, Taylor's reference to the brand's similarities with Adidas cannot be ignored. In January 2023, the German sportswear giants lost a legal dispute with Thom Browne over their usage of the four-stripe design - a trademark feature of what Parker has sported on the touchline.

Adidas planned to claim Thom Browne were required to pay approximately £6.4m in damages, but were unsuccessful.

The former Hammers midfielder is set to become the club's latest head coach after Nuno Espirito Santo's dismissal

Ultimately, what Parker dons in the dugout will not be his highest priority at the London Stadium, as West Ham are desperate to return to the Premier League instantly.

The Hammers are currently eighth after 14 games played despite scoring the most goals out of any side in the Championship, having won just two of their last six games.

West Ham fans, is Scott Parker the right man to lead your club forward? Comment below!

But, they are only five points behind leaders, Middlesbrough, with 32 games remaining, starting with Saturday's trip to Blackburn Rovers.

#2236. Fenerbahce 3-2 Liverpool: Trey Nyoni goal sets record but Champions League defeat reveals January transfer window reality

By Lewis Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 01:26

Liverpool midfielder Trey Nyoni became the youngest player in the club's history to score his first goal for the team in a European Cup match (19 years, 127 days).

Though the midfielder excelled once again, his side ultimately lost 3-2 against Fenerbahce on Wednesday, with the Reds suffering their first Champions League loss of the campaign.

Andoni Iraola was frustrated by how his side conceded all three goals, arguing that they should have done more to prevent their opponents scoring from set pieces.

If there was a positive for the Reds, it would be that Nyoni managed to net his first goal for the club, opening the scoring in the 25th minute.

Opta revealed that the teenager became the youngest player to score their first goal for Liverpool in the Champions League or European Cup.

19 - Trey Nyoni is the youngest player in Liverpool's history to score his first goal for the club in a European Cup match (19 years, 127 days). Kicks. https://t.co/9Hb4p0SnBx

Bournemouth's Alex Scott has played under Iraola, but given he has been linked with Chelsea, Liverpool should be ready to move onto a different target.

#2235. How long West Brom face without George Campbell as James Morrison confirms injury extent

West Brom host Millwall in the Championship this Saturday, and they will hope to extend their league unbeaten run to six games. However, WBA will have to do so without club captain George Campbell, who withdrew from the Southampton draw after feeling his groin.

Now, ahead of the Millwall tie, Baggies boss James Morrison has confirmed Campbell will be absent against the Lions. Speaking to the club’s official media, he said: “Unfortunately George picked up a groin injury against Southampton and we expect him to be out for between ten to 12 days. It’s not ideal, but it could’ve been worse. Hopefully it’ll just be one game that he misses.”

Campbell has played 15 times in all competitions this season, sitting unused on the bench on only one occasion. As captain, he serves as a key presence at centre-back, so while it is a blow to lose him, there will be relief that he isn’t out for an extended spell.

There’s no hiding that Campbell is a loss for Albion. He was instrumental in their survival turnaround last season and emerged as a real leader, earning the captain’s armband and retaining a key role for the 2026/27 campaign.

Thankfully for West Brom and Morrison, he has a straightforward selection decision when dealing with Campbell’s absence. Nat Phillips replaced him at the break on Tuesday night, and he will more than likely do so from the start against Millwall.

In the two Championship games Campbell has not started, it has been fellow centre-back Chris Mepham who has stepped in as captain. As such, he seems the likely candidate to do so against the Lions, although Jayson Molumby has donned the armband before as well.

Morrison will be hoping his remaining WBA centre-backs can avoid suspension or injury, as only Mepham and Phillips remain with Campbell sidelined.

Campbell was called up for the United States national team for their November fixtures. But, with the extent of his injury now confirmed, it will be expected that he withdraws.

That will give the West Brom man the break to recover from his groin issue and work back to fitness for the return to club football. WBA face Swansea City in the first game back, and the hope is that Campbell will be fit.

Morrison and the physios will be sure to be careful with Campbell to ensure he doesn’t suffer a further setback. Losing him for one game is a blow, but seeing him cast to the sidelines for longer would spark real concern given the Baggies’ lack of centre-back depth.

#2233. Predicting Thomas Tuchel's England squad for Croatia, Spain UEFA Nations League fixtures

By Ben Sully | 05 Nov 2026 02:38, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 03:32

England will complete the 2026-27 Nations League league phase with fixtures against Croatia and Spain this month.

The Three Lions will welcome Croatia to Wembley on November 12 before they head to Atletico Madrid's Metropolitano for a test against the world champions on November 15.

Thomas Tuchel's side head into the final two fixtures in second place in Group A3 after taking nine points from their first four matches since finishing in third position at the 2026 World Cup.

After starting with a narrow 3-2 defeat against Spain at Wembley, England recorded three consecutive victories by a combined 12-0 scoreline to all but seal a quarter-final berth.

The Three Lions will look to mathematically secure a last-eight spot and challenge Spain for the honour of clinching first place.

Ahead of Tuchel's squad announcement on Friday, November 6, Sports Mole predicts which players will make the cut for the final two matches of the Nations League league phase.

There appears to be little uncertainty surrounding the first two names on what is expected to be a three-man list of goalkeepers.

Everton’s Jordan Pickford and Leeds United’s James Trafford are guaranteed to receive the call-up from Tuchel, with the real focus on whether Tuchel will pick a number one for this international window or share the game time like he did in the opening four Nations League matches.

Pickford has long been seen as England’s number one, but he had to watch the opening two games against Spain and the Czech Republic from the bench as Tuchel offered Trafford the chance to make his case to be his first-choice goalkeeper.

Trafford then made way for Pickford to feature in the 7-0 away win over Croatia and the 3-0 home victory against the Czech Republic.

While his position may not be as certain as it once was, Pickford continues to close in on a century of England caps, with the 32-year-old just seven appearances away from reaching that impressive milestone.

In regard to the third-choice goalkeeper, Dean Henderson is set to get the nod over Brighton & Hove Albion’s Jason Steele after recently making his return to action from an ankle injury.

Predicted England goalkeepers for upcoming Nations League fixtures: Dean Henderson, Jordan Pickford, James Trafford

Tuchel initially picked nine defenders for the last international window before Newcastle United’s Tino Livramento pulled out due to injury.

Livramento remains unavailable for selection, along with England’s first-choice right-back Reece James, who is expected to be out until the new year after undergoing knee surgery.

In more positive news, Djed Spence, who was one of the standout players of England’s World Cup campaign, is back in contention after making six appearances for Inter Milan since returning from injury.

Trent Alexander-Arnold, meanwhile, has struggled for game time in recent weeks due to competition from Federico Valverde and Denzel Dumfries.

However, he could still get the nod for England after impressing in his two appearances in the last international window.

Jarell Quansah can also cover at right-back as well as offering an option in central defence, while Lewis Hall and Nico O’Reilly are likely to be the two left-backs in the squad.

Ezri Konsa, Marc Guehi and Jarrad Branthwaite are widely expected to be among Tuchel’s central defensive options, while injuries to John Stones and Trevoh Chalobah could open the door for Levi Colwill, who has started eight of Chelsea’s nine Premier League games this season.

Dan Burn is another name who could be in the mix, but while he played a useful role at the World Cup, Tuchel may be reluctant to bring the 34-year-old back into the fold as he keeps one eye on his plans for the European Championship in 2028.

Predicted England defenders for upcoming Nations League fixtures: Trent Alexander-Arnold, Jarrad Branthwaite, Levi Colwill, Marc Guehi, Lewis Hall, Ezri Konsa, Nico O’Reilly, Jarell Quansah, Djed Spence

The main headline news for England's midfield options is the imminent return of Declan Rice, who missed the last international window due to load management.

Rice is expected to form a central midfield pairing with Elliot Anderson, a player who continues to grow week by week under Enzo Maresca at Manchester City.

Myles Lewis-Skelly started three of the first four Nations League fixtures in Rice's absence, suggesting that Tuchel sees him more as a central midfielder than a left-back.

The Arsenal man should make the cut again, although he may have to settle for a substitute role this time around.

Alex Scott is someone who is almost definitely set to miss out on selection despite impressing in his 70-minute display against Croatia last month.

The Bournemouth star picked up a thigh injury in the emphatic win and has since missed seven games for the Cherries.

While he is close to a return to action, the November international window will come too soon, which may open the door for Jordan Henderson to make the squad for the first time since suffering a wrist injury at the World Cup.

James Garner was called up as a replacement for Kobbie Mainoo in September, and he could retain his spot ahead of the Manchester United midfielder, who has only recently returned from injury.

Looking at attacking midfielders, Jude Bellingham will be one of the first names on Tuchel's list, and he is set to join the national team in a positive mood after scoring in Real Madrid's last two matches.

Morgan Gibbs-White was a late call-up for the last squad, but is likely to miss out this time due to his underwhelming form in a struggling Nottingham Forest side.

There is a possibility that Tuchel may look to the future with Fulham star Josh King, who is an outside contender for a call-up after firmly establishing himself as a key player at Fulham this term.

Predicted England midfielders for upcoming Nations League fixtures: Elliot Anderson, Jude Bellingham, James Garner, Jordan Henderson, Myles Lewis-Skelly, Declan Rice

The front three of Bukayo Saka, Anthony Gordon and Harry Kane scored 11 goals between them across the first four UEFA Nations League fixtures as the trio linked up to clinical effect.

Unfortunately, it is a forward line that is guaranteed to be broken following the news that Saka is set for a spell on the sidelines with a thigh injury.

Noni Madueke has stepped up for Arsenal in Saka’s absence and is likely to be asked to do the same for England’s final two league phase matches.

Madueke’s Arsenal teammate Eberechi Eze is someone who appears to be trusted by Tuchel and should be on the list when the squad announcement is made on Friday, especially as another left-sided option, Marcus Rashford, is unavailable due to injury.

Liverpool’s exciting youngster Rio Ngumoha is also set to make the cut after making his first two competitive international appearances in the away and home games against the Czech Republic.

Meanwhile, Chelsea’s Morgan Rogers will be full of confidence when he joins the England squad after netting three goals in his previous five appearances.

Rogers appears to be stealing the limelight from his friend and teammate Cole Palmer, who appeared to frustrate Tuchel when he pulled out of his most recent squad with a minor issue.

The England boss may be tempted to offer Palmer another chance, especially with Saka sidelined, but it is clear that he has doubts over the strength of the 24-year-old’s desire to play for his country.

In regard to the centre-forward spot, England captain Harry Kane is a guaranteed starter and, barring injury, he will break his country’s all-time appearance record against Croatia at Wembley.

The biggest question is who will provide cover for the 2026 Ballon d’Or winner, with England struggling to find someone who can get anywhere near the level Kane produces.

Calvert-Lewin is not operating at the same elite level as Kane, but he has scored four goals in nine Premier League appearances this season and is likely to be the backup striker for the upcoming international window.

Tuchel is expected to only pick two strikers, but if he wants to add a third to his squad, he could look to the Championship where Middlesbrough’s Will Lankshear is the leading scorer with 13 goals to his name.

The in-form striker has been a regular for the England Under-21s, but with qualifying for next summer’s Under-21 European Championship all wrapped up, it could be a good opportunity to offer him a taste of life in the senior setup.

Predicted England attackers for upcoming Nations League fixtures: Dominic Calvert-Lewin, Eberechi Eze, Anthony Gordon, Harry Kane, Noni Madueke, Rio Ngumoha, Cole Palmer, Morgan Rogers

#2232. QPR confirm Sky Sports decision and it's left a lot of R's fans unhappy

Sky Sports' decision to move EFL games for TV coverage hardly ever goes down well with fans, and the new year's announcement of the live picks has frustrated some QPR supporters.

It's been a solid opening few months for Julien Stephan's side this season. The Hoops have dropped out of the top eight after just one win in their last four, but they resided exclusively in the play-offs before then.

And, with that one win in that run coming by way of a 5-0 rout against former league leaders Swansea City, there's still enough to suggest that they could be in the mix for the postseason come May.

Collect every point to win the round. Don’t let Neil Warnock catch you.

Arrow keys or WASD to move. P to pause, M to mute.

Swipe on the pitch or use the pad to move.

To do so, the fanbase will want as many games at Loftus Road to take place either in the evening or at the traditional time of 3pm to help build the atmosphere on one of the tighter grounds in the Championship, and for away games not to be at awkward times, too, so they can back the squad on the road.

However, three of QPR's home games from January to the end of February against Birmingham City, Southampton and West Ham United have all been moved to a 12:30pm kickoff on a Saturday.

Meanwhile, Julien Stephan's side will travel to Lincoln City on a Friday night in late January, which is not just a six-hour round trip with no traffic, but comes off the back of an away day in Burnley just three days earlier.

It's safe to say that R's fans have taken umbrage at the decision to move those games for TV coverage.

"How can they move Lincoln to the Friday when we play away on the Tuesday that week? Absolute farce," one fan commented in response to the decision. "3 of the best home games at 12:30 zombie hour," another said.

"Well these are awful. 3 home 1230s. Away at Lincoln Friday night after being away at Burnley on the Tuesday," was also among the replies, combining the two.

"Absolutely dreadful selections for us. 3 home games and a decent away day ruined. No football fans that I know enjoy the 12.30pm kick offs so why are they doing this to the game?" another fan asked.

And a fifth simply said, sarcastically, "Cheers Sky, thanks… The one game we didn’t want moved to a Friday night."

The movement of games against Birmingham, Southampton and West Ham, three sides currently in the top eight and who are expected to stay there come the new year, indicates that Sky Sports are hoping to televise some potential clashes between sides in the top eight.

It's a credit to how well QPR are currently doing under Julien Stephan, but it could be detrimental to their performances in those games.

As mentioned, Loftus Road can be quite a difficult place to go when the crowd are raring to go and up for the game. Swansea know how that feels, as do Derby County and, while in a draw, Preston North End, who allowed chance after chance and squandered a 2-0 lead despite having a man advantage.

How detrimental will the kick-off time move be for QPR in those three home games? Let us know what you think in the comments.

In these big matchups against fellow strong-starters who have the spending power that QPR don't, the Hoops are going to have to rely on those in the stands to level the playing field and push them towards a win.

At 12:30, it can often take a little while to heat up, which may be more of a benefit to the away sides, who could take advantage of a quieter-than-usual Loftus Road.

When pairing that home lunchtime triple-header with a trip to Lincoln, the one away day that the majority of Championship mainstays were looking forward to doing, given how long it's been since the Imps were in the division, there's arguably no positive to these TV selections.

That being said, though, QPR won't be the only side aggrieved by having their schedule switched to accommodate the cameras. In fact, it's almost a given that every side has some grumbles about those timings being changed.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

On the bright side, at least there isn't a 12:30 away from home. People travelling down from Birmingham or up from Southampton to Loftus Road for those meetings may have it a little harder due to the early wake-up.

QPR will at least be hoping that this decent start can be maintained for the rest of the calendar year, so the decisions to move these games for a national audience can be justified.

#2231. Harry Kane makes huge career call as Premier League record dream fades

Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane says he feels “right at home” after signing a contract extension at Bayern Munich.

The England captain, 33, took the game’s top individual honour last week after a record-breaking stint with the Bundesliga giants and has now recommitted to the club until June 2029.

Since arriving from Tottenham in the summer of 2023, Kane has scored a remarkable 156 goals in 161 appearances, winning two league titles and three domestic cups.

Speaking after penning his new deal, he said: “I feel right at home at FC Bayern. I love the club, the fans and the city of Munich.

“We have a fantastic team and, in Vincent Kompany, a fantastic manager. It makes me very proud to be part of this club. My whole family is very happy here. I was keen to stay. I’m now looking forward to the future and I want to win many more titles with this great club.”

Scotland boss hails 'buy-in' as senior trio embrace Under-21 drop

Erling Haaland is Manchester City’s hero again in entertaining win in Leipzig

Liverpool lead twice before losing to Fenerbahce in Champions League

The move effectively ends the prospect of Kane returning to England to take on Alan Shearer’s Premier League goals record of 260. Kane sits second, having hit 213 for Tottenham and has previously been linked with a homecoming with Spurs as well as a move to Manchester United. By the end of his contract at the Allianz Arena, he will be almost 36.

Herbert Hainer, Bayern president, was thrilled to tie down the striker, adding: “What began as a major transfer has, over time, become a special chapter in FC Bayern’s history.

“Harry Kane lives up to every promise football has to offer and he has secured his place in the hearts of the fans as one of the greatest figures in the sport of our time. This is a special moment for FC Bayern today.”

Sporting director Christoph Freund said: “We’re absolutely delighted because with Harry Kane we have the Ballon d’Or winner on the pitch – a team player, a leader, the best all-round striker in the world.

“We all benefit from his qualities as a player and as a person, day in, day out. FC Bayern thrives on players who never stop giving their all, even when they have already achieved the greatest of goals.”

#2230. Willem II vs PEC Zwolle - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 04:34

Willem II and PEC Zwolle kick off round 12 of the 2026-27 Eredivisie season with a clash at the Koning Willem II Stadion on Friday night.

The hosts are still searching for their first league win of the campaign, while the visitors aim to extend their unbeaten league run to four matches.

Willem II begin life under Jurgen Streppel after the 57-year-old’s recent appointment as head coach, and they will hope their new manager can hit the ground running, especially given their sluggish start to the season.

De Superkruiken claimed their first win of 2026-27 with a 4-3 triumph over Helmond Sport in the KNVB Beker, but then suffered a humiliating 7-0 defeat to reigning champions PSV Eindhoven—taking their loss tally to nine in 11 Eredivisie outings.

Already 11 points adrift of safety, Willem II are paying the price for one of the longest winless starts to an Eredivisie season. As the campaign enters its second third, they require a dramatic turnaround in fortunes to avoid the drop.

Three points against Zwolle would be the perfect start to Streppel’s reign, but Friday’s hosts have struggled at home against their upcoming opponents, winning just one of the last five meetings (L3, D1).

With 26 of their 39 goals conceded coming after half-time, Willem II’s fate could well be determined by their second-half performance. The prospect of facing a team that has scored just a third of its 12 goals in the opening 45 minutes offers little encouragement.

Though not as poor as Willem II, Zwolle also made a poor start to their league campaign, securing just one win from their opening eight matches. However, they have since won two of their last three, either side of a 1-1 draw with Utrecht.

Sandwiched between those three unbeaten league matches was a 2-1 cup defeat to lower-league opposition - arguably the most disappointing result of the season for De Blauwvingers. Nonetheless, they travel to Tilburg with renewed confidence.

Zwolle remain in search of a new head coach following Henry van den Vegt’s departure. In the meantime, caretaker manager Dennis van der Ree is gradually making a case for himself, as his tenure has coincided with an upturn in results.

A victory over Willem II in their final match before the international break would undoubtedly strengthen calls from the Zwolle fanbase for Van der Ree to be appointed permanently. The 47-year-old will be eager to bolster his reputation after a difficult spell with Groningen.

No team in the Eredivisie has converted a lower percentage of their big chances than Friday’s visitors (20.8%), highlighting a key area for improvement if they are to sustain their upturn in form.

Jaden Slory served a one-match suspension during Willem II’s defeat to PSV last time out, but the 21-year-old is available for selection here.

This leaves Justin Hoogma and Amine Lackhar, currently sidelined with knee and groin injuries, respectively, as the hosts’ two guaranteed absences.

Eighteen-year-old Givaro Rahajaan has not featured in a matchday squad since early September and remains out of contention for PEC Zwolle.

Sherel Floranus will miss a fourth consecutive match for the visitors, who are also without Filip Krastev due to an Achilles injury.

Younes Namli found the net in the most recent meeting between these sides, and the Dane will be aiming to make an impact once again.

Delanghe; Ter Avest, Stam, Dekker, Tjoe a On; Twigt, Zarrouk; Slory, Van Aalst, Van Crooy; Haen

Schendelaar; Van de Haar, Viergever, Graves, Aertssen; Sommer, Thomas, Van der Wal; Mbayo, Van Hooijdonk, Namli

Back in the Eredivisie after 11 years, new Willem II boss Streppel is tasked with pulling off what would be one of the biggest escapes in league history.

Given Zwolle’s current form and Willem II’s ongoing defensive frailties, the visitors are narrowly tipped to come out on top.
