#2229. British football's top 25 most demanding football fanbases ranked by AI

The United Kingdom is the birthplace of football and is widely acknowledged as the home of the world's most popular sport, and, naturally, some fanbases are proud, passionate, and above all, demanding.

The first laws of the game of football were established by the FA in 1863, and in the more than 160 years since, the sport has developed and refined drastically into the spectacle we all know and love today.

Certain towns and cities across the UK live and breathe football, as how their team does at the weekend will largely affect their moods for the rest of the week until they play again, and, as a byproduct of that, some teams' supporters demand and expect more from their side.

Whether you're a fan of a League Two club or one in the Premier League, your expectations and demands will naturally vary depending on who you support, as Football League World has asked AI to rank the 25 most demanding clubs in the United Kingdom right now.

AI says: Wolves supporters expect the club to be an established Premier League side, competing towards the top half rather than simply fighting to stay up.

One of the founding members of the EFL, Wolverhampton Wanderers are engulfed in a rich history dating back to 1877, and in the near-150 years since their inception, the Old Gold have taken home 17 major domestic trophies, including 11 league titles, four FA Cups, and two league cups.

Success hasn't found its way to Molineux often in modern years, but still a regular in the Premier League until recently, the Old Gold unsurprisingly snuck their way into this list with some of the most demanding fans in the UK.

AI says: As former Premier League champions, Blackburn supporters expect their club to return to the top flight and compete at a much higher level.

Blackburn Rovers are another one of the founding members of the EFL, and established in 1875, the Lancashire-based outfit is the most successful club in Lancashire in terms of major honours, with 11 in their cabinet, including the 1994/95 Premier League title.

Although the club has spent the bulk of the last decade or so in the second tier, their rich history has naturally meant their fanbase demands and expects more than what has been afforded to them in recent times.

AI says: Bolton's successful Premier League years have left supporters expecting a return to the upper reaches of English football.

Founded in 1874 and another founding member of the EFL, Bolton Wanderers have spent a large chunk of their recent history bouncing between the bottom three divisions, but have spent a good amount of time in the Premier League since its reformatting in 1992.

The Trotters have four FA Cups in their trophy haul, and though three came before World War 2, their fanbase remains proud of their history and demands more as one of Greater Manchester's biggest clubs.

AI says: Derby are a historically significant club, and their supporters expect regular promotion challenges and, ultimately, a return to the Premier League.

As one of four EFL founding members in a row, Derby County boast an extremely proud and passionate fanbase dating back to 1884, and with three major honours in their trophy haul, they have the history to back it up.

The Rams have spent just seven years in the Premier League since its inception in 1992, and given the club's status in England, supporters would surely have been demanding more.

AI says: Coventry supporters increasingly expect the club to establish itself in the Premier League rather than settle for occasional success in the Championship.

Coventry City are a Premier League club once again, but go back less than a decade, the Sky Blues were dwindling in League Two on the brink of existence.

With one FA Cup in their major honours list, Coventry don't have as rich a history as other clubs on this list, but boast an incredibly proud and passionate support few clubs in the country can rival, as AI believes they deserve a spot in this ranking.

AI says: Everton's history and stature mean supporters expect European qualification and considerably more than mid-table mediocrity.

Since the Premier League's inception, Everton have never spent a single season outside of the top tier, despite a few close calls, and with 15 major honours in their history, the Toffees have one of the UK's proudest and most loyal supporters.

Largely in the shadow of their noisy neighbours Liverpool throughout most of their history, Everton have held their own, though having seen the red side of Merseyside boast the majority of success over the years, supporters have naturally demanded more from their club.

AI says: West Ham supporters expect European football and believe the club has the resources, history and fanbase to achieve more.

West Ham United are back in the Championship right now, but Hammers supporters will be hoping, and demanding, that it won't be for the long haul, and Scott Parker can take them straight back to the Premier League.

With average attendances rivalling the very best in the entire UK, and even the rest of Europe, despite their second tier status, West Ham's fanbase is something to be marvelled at, and with five major honours to boot, it's hardly a surprise their fans demand more than struggling in the second tier.

AI says: Middlesbrough supporters expect their club to be challenging for promotion virtually every season, given its history as a regular Premier League side.

Middlesbrough have spent nearly a decade in the second tier, and though they will be hoping to change that under Kim Hellberg this year, the Teesside outfit can still boast 15 years in the top flight, as well as one League Cup.

A consistent presence in the Championship promotion picture, Middlesbrough fans often demand their side to be fighting towards the top of the league year after year, as AI gives them a big ranking in this list.

AI says: West Brom are traditionally viewed as a top-flight club, meaning anything short of a serious promotion challenge can be considered disappointing.

Established in 1878 and another founding member of the EFL, West Bromwich Albion are the other side of the Black Country to Wolves, and like their Old Gold neighbours, boast a rich and proud history consisting of seven major honours.

The Baggies have spent the bulk of the millennium between the Premier League and Championship, with their support demanding to, at the very least, be challenging for a place in the top flight year-on-year if they are not already in it.

AI says: Sheffield United supporters expect Premier League football and believe their club belongs among England's established top-flight sides.

Now to the city of Sheffield, as Sheffield United take their place on this list with five major honours across their history.

Their demanding support largely stems from wanting to be ahead of bitter city rivals Sheffield Wednesday at all times; if the Blades are behind the Owls at any point, supporters are quick to point that out and demand better.

#2228. Birmingham City team news and predicted XI to face Swansea City: August Priske remains a doubt

Birmingham City welcome Swansea City to St Andrew’s this weekend looking to strengthen their bid for automatic promotion. The Blues currently sit 3rd in the Championship table with 26 points from 14 games, level with Stoke City and just two points behind leaders Middlesbrough.

Chris Davies’ side head into the contest in excellent form after a convincing 3-0 win over Millwall last time out. Alex Cochrane, Max Bird and Jay Stansfield all got on the scoresheet in the first half, while summer arrival Liam Millar has also made an impact with four goals to his name.

Swansea City are also firmly in the promotion hunt, occupying 5th place with 26 points from their opening 14 fixtures. Vitor Matos has enjoyed an encouraging start to his tenure in South Wales, with the Swans demonstrating plenty of quality throughout the campaign.

However, the visitors will be keen to bounce back after suffering a damaging 3-1 defeat away at Portsmouth. Swansea looked to have rescued a point after fighting back from a goal down, but two goals in stoppage time, arriving in the 92nd and 98th minutes, condemned them to defeat.

August Priske is a major doubt for Birmingham City’s Championship clash with Swansea City this weekend, with the striker set to be assessed on Friday before a decision is made on his availability, as reported by Birmingham Live.

The Dane missed Tuesday night’s 3-0 victory over Millwall after picking up an injury during the previous weekend’s meeting with Lincoln City. Priske has scored seven goals this season and would be a significant absence for Davies’ side.

The Blues boss provided a positive update but admitted it remains too early to know whether his leading scorer will feature. He said: “He’s doing OK. He’s made progress. It was a heavy knock that he took and he’s made progress but it’s probably too early to say.”

Davies added that it would be a close call, with Birmingham hoping to get Priske involved in some capacity. Paik Seung-ho and Dael Fry could return to the squad, while goalkeeper James Beadle is expected to miss out again.

Allsop; Laird, Neumann, Klarer, Cochrane; Solis, Iwata; Roberts, Bird, Millar; Stansfield

After the performance against Millwall in midweek will likely see Davies keep the same team. The only change may depend on Priske’s fitness.

Should Priske be fit then Bird will likely drop out of the side with Stansfield sitting deeper. However, both Bird and Stansfield impressed in midweek so it would be harsh to drop them.

Ryan Allsop will keep his place between the sticks with Beadle remaining out. While Fry and Paik will start from the bench should they return to the squad.

#2225. ‘I don’t want him to leave’ – West Brom star dubbed the ‘best’ in the league

EFL pundit Tom Garratt has claimed West Brom’s Ousmane Diakite is the best holding midfielder in the Championship.

Speaking on the latest episode of the Pyramid Podcast that analysed all the midweek games in the second tier, Garratt reacted alongside co-host Callum Fowler to Albion’s 1-1 draw with Southampton on Tuesday evening, which was dubbed a fantastic advert for the Championship by Baggies boss James Morrison.

It was a tale of two halves in the Black Country, with Morrison’s men dominating Southampton throughout the first 45 minutes, as an Isaac Price penalty saw them go into the break with the lead.

But the Saints, who were so poor in the first half, turned up the heat in the second, going on to have the majority of possession and chances, as Samuel Edozie’s close-range effort got them back on level terms a matter of minutes into the second period.

The Baggies will have been disappointed to not have snatched all three points in the latter stages themselves though, as Aune Heggebo’s close-range effort was denied well by Daniel Peretz, while Tammer Bany clipped the outside of the post with a low-driven effort on a late Albion counter-attack.

Overall, a point was a fair result between two top Championship sides, who both should be in promotion contention come the end of the season.

And one player who really stood out in the game was Ousmane Diakite, with EFL pundit Tom Garratt making quite the claim about the man from Mali.

In a topsy-turvy game in B71 on Tuesday evening, while most West Brom supporters will have been content with a point, there will have been disappointment that they didn’t claim maximum points following a dominant first half and the opportunities they carved out late into the second.

But to have that feeling against a side that would have been top of the division without their four-point deduction at the start of the campaign, it’s a world apart from where Albion were just a few months ago under James Morrison, with the Black Country outfit scraping their way to survival.

These 10 players won't be available as West Brom take on Millwall this Saturday

It just goes to show how far the team and club have come in such a short space of time, and so much credit has to go to Morrison, who is getting unbelievable amounts out of his current squad, which has quite a few players in it who are experiencing the rough and tumble of the Championship for the very first time.

Playing a side like Southampton off the park for the first 45 minutes and forcing them to not even have a shot just goes to show that Albion can mix it with the very best in the division, and it was a real statement performance that they definitely are in the crop of teams that stand every chance of gaining promotion back to the Premier League.

Speaking on the latest episode of the Pyramid Podcast, (from 34:06), West Brom fan and EFL pundit Tom Garratt has claimed Ousmane Diakite is the best holding midfielder in the division following his performance against Southampton.

The Mali international was a titanic force in the middle of the park against the Saints, making 11 recoveries and six tackles, while possessing the pace to drive Albion up the pitch and make six line-breaking passes on the night, as per stats from FotMob.

Garratt is fearful that Albion could lose their star man if he keeps up these types of displays, while being shocked at the progress he’s made under Morrison's guidance.

Garratt said: “Say it quietly, because I don’t want him (Diakite) to leave, we might have the best holding midfielder in the league. He’s a joke mate.

“It’s been his third season now, but when he came in, I thought he was a donkey, like a donkey, and he’s just fast, he’s powerful, you can’t get the ball off him, he’s technically getting better and better each week, like his passing range is really improving.

“Molumby was excellent as well, and they really complement each other well.”

It was an awesome display from Ousmane Diakite against Southampton, who has come back with a vengeance after dropping out of the team in recent times.

Diakite was dropped to the bench for clashes against Wrexham and Blackburn Rovers as Toby Collyer stepped into the fray, but an injury suffered by the former Manchester United man allowed Diakite to get his place back in the starting XI, and he’s grabbed the opportunity firmly with both hands.

West Brom fans, how impressed have you been with Ousmane Diakite so far this season? How important is he to help maintain your promotion hopes? Comment your thoughts below!

His performance against the Saints was a reflection of how far he’s come under James Morrison, with the current Albion boss the only man in the dugout to really hand Diakite a starting role on a regular basis, with much of his time at The Hawthorns seeing him be an impact player off the bench.

But he’s now proving why he’s one of the top performing players in the squad, with Diakite adding so much energy and aggressiveness with his defending, while he then shows bursts of pace to break into more advanced areas and setup teammates for chances with some cutting-edge passing.

The 26-year-old really does have so much to his game at both ends of the pitch, and it’s coming out in abundance now his confidence levels are skyrocketing.

Baggies supporters will be amazed at what they’re seeing from Diakite, who has gone from what looked to be a complete flop into a Championship superstar.

Diakite will be hopeful of another influential performance when Albion host Millwall this weekend before the international break.

#2223. Cagliari vs. Frosinone - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 05 Nov 2026 05:57

Winless at Cagliari since 2013, Frosinone head into Saturday's gameweek-11 fixture at Unipol Domus as the likely favourites.

The promoted side have thrived since returning to the top flight, sitting sixth in the Serie A table ahead of another visit to a ground where they have not won in over a decade.

Cagliari's start to the 2026-27 campaign has been mixed, with the Sardinians experiencing contrasting fortunes across the opening rounds.

Fabio Pisacane's men claimed four victories from their opening five matches, suffering an understandable home defeat to Inter Milan, before drawing 1-1 with Juventus in gameweek six.

That point appeared encouraging in isolation, but the Isolani have failed to collect any more since, suffering four consecutive defeats to Monza, Bologna, Roma and Lazio while notably surrendering leads against both capital clubs.

Those results leave Pisacane's men 12th in the Serie A table, although they could climb as high as eighth if the teams above them, Napoli, Bologna, Torino and Genoa, all drop points.

However, that outcome depends on the Sardinians ending their league losing streak against an in-form, high-flying visiting side.

Frosinone's return to the top flight after two years brought the customary uncertainty, but the Canaries have flourished in the big time.

They offered signs of what was to come during their opening-day defeat to Juventus at Stadio Benito Stirpe, where the Old Lady claimed a 1-0 victory.

Only Napoli have beaten Massimiliano Alvini's side since, with the Canarini securing six victories from their next nine matches to sit sixth in the Serie A table ahead of matchday 11.

Their only away league defeat remains last month's 3-0 hammering at the Maradona in Naples, while the Ciociari have claimed a 3-0 victory over Fiorentina and earned satisfying draws at Genoa and Atalanta, the latter finishing 4-4 in Bergamo.

If there were grounds for criticism of Alvini's troops, it would be their failure to capitalise on Genoa and Atalanta's underwhelming form when facing those clubs, with Daniele De Rossi's men sitting 15th in the table and the Bergamo outfit two places below.

Nonetheless, they enter Saturday's encounter seeking their first away top-flight victory since August, a result that would also end their 13-year wait for success at Cagliari.

Adam Obert will miss this weekend's clash after receiving a late red card for two bookable offences against Lazio last time out.

Alessandro Deiola and Roberto Gagliardini are doubtful due to head and ankle injuries respectively; however, Alieu Fadera (thigh), Mattia Felici (knee), Riyad Idrissi (knee) and M'Bala Nzola (thigh) are unavailable.

With two-goal forward Nzola absent, Daniel Maldini is the only available player to have scored more than once for the Sardinians this term and will aim to add to his five-goal tally against the visitors.

Antonio Raimondo was withdrawn after 30 minutes of Frosinone's eventual 2-0 victory over Torino, and the Capocannoniere contender is unavailable for the away side.

Raimondo's absence represents a significant blow, given that his eight goals account for a third of the Canaries' league tally of 24; Daniel Birligea should start, having recently scored twice in the 5-1 thrashing of Lecce on October 29.

Florian Grillitsch is also likely to miss out for the Canaries, having not played since September due to a thigh injury.

Caprile; Ze Pedro, Mina, Rodriguez, Sugawara; Adopo, Romano; Koffer, Maldini, Fazzini; Mendy

Palmisani; Oyono, Monterisi, Calvani, Bracaglia; Masini, Calo; Ghedjemis, Schmid, Kvernadze; Birligea

Raimondo's absence removes a significant source of goals for Frosinone, but Birligea's recent brace against Lecce suggests that the visitors retain enough firepower to trouble their struggling hosts.

Cagliari have repeatedly failed to protect promising positions during their four-match losing run, and the Canaries have our backing to end their long wait for victory in Sardinia.

#2222. GdS: How Amorim’s fluidity helped Rabiot record career-best start to a season

Adrien Rabiot continues to deliver in the biggest games for AC Milan, and Ruben Amorim is arguably getting the best ever form out of him.

La Gazzetta dello Sport begin a report by recalling how different things were in the summer. With Milan falling to fifth and Massmiliano Allegri being sacked, many presumed that Rabiot could follow him to Napoli, to play Champions League football.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Rabiot not only remained at Milanello, but is also the Atlas of the team this year. He carries it on his shoulders and takes its weight with disarming ease and personality. While Allegri observes Milan from below, Adrien has found a new coach who is giving him a new lease of life.

The derby goal is an excellent indicator of Rabiot’s confidence, attempting that billiard-like shot (even though some, like Yunus Musah, maintain it was a cross) in a match like this, with the game still wide open, signifies complete self-confidence.

The Frenchman knows perfectly well he has a powerful shot, but he opted for finesse, guiding the ball to kiss the post and land in the back of the net as eleven Nerazzurri players watched it slowly and mockingly cross their penalty area.

Rabiot was one of the derby’s heroes but has been performing at a high level for several matches now. He currently has the best average rating in the squad, and it’s a stratospheric 6.81 (out of 10, across all games).

The other key number is nine: five goals and four assists this season makes nine contributions, the most in the squad. In the league, he’s been involved in a goal in each of his last five games: assists against Lazio, goals against Lecce and Sassuolo, a goal and assist against Atalanta, and a goal in the derby.

To better understand his further improvement from an already very high level, we must consider that in the entire last season, Adrien had six goals and four assists. He’s one goal away from matching that tally.

Some tactical considerations help contextualise things. Compared to Allegri, Amorim’s style of football is on another level, being much more attacking. It’s not based solely on counter-attacks, but on a flow of play that starts from midfield and allows for the runs of those who move in between the lines.

Rabiot has always been a player who can get forward: he has the physique, technique, and footballing intelligence. Amorim has given him a further step because he’s made him more difficult to read for opponents.

If before, the Frenchman was a classic pivot midfielder, now you find him practically everywhere. At the start of the season, Amorim’s style of football was positional, locked into a 3-4-2-1 that seemed set in stone with Rabiot behind the striker.

Then the Portuguese coach realised he was doing himself harm and began a tactical overhaul. The fundamentalist has become fluid, and the playing system is now an abstract concept that must be filled with individual interpretations.

This is a set of circumstances where Rabiot shines. The derby is a perfect example: the former PSG man was everywhere, depending on his own tendencies and the team’s need for certain characteristics in certain parts if the pitch.

This is the best start to a season of his career. Now, in addition to the pitch, he’ll also have to keep an eye on his desk. After Strahinja Pavlovic and Christian Pulisic, he’s the next one destined to ascend to the fourth floor of Casa Milan to sign a renewal waiting for him.

#2220. Port Vale confirm TV decision and one thing is causing a real backlash

Port Vale supporters have learned the new Sky Sports TV schedule just after the new year, and it would be to suggest that it has not gone down particularly well amongst the fanbase.

Port Vale, after a tricky start back to life in League Two following relegation from League One last year, are starting to finally starting to find their footing, as Jon Brady's side aim to claw their way back up the table.

There remains hope amongst the Valiants that this year could have a dream conclusion, though for those that often travel away with the club, they have just received some bad news.

Which sets of EFL fans have the most misery induced by their clubs?

Port Vale has confirmed via X that some fixtures around January and February 2027 have been moved to fit in with Sky Sports' TV coverage, with one of those being the away trip to Exeter City.

The initial kick-off time at St James' Park was a standard 3 pm on Saturday, February 20, but now that has been brought forward by two-and-a-half hours to a 12:30 pm kick-off time, giving Vale supporters an incredibly early morning to look forward to.

From Vale Park in Stoke-on-Trent, it's a near four-hour trip down to Devon without any stops and perfect traffic, as now supporters will have no choice but to get an even earlier night to be wide awake for the trip down south.

Naturally, this news hasn't gone down well with Vale fans, who have taken to X to react to the update.

An eight-hour round journey, at best, is already difficult enough as it is, but make that an early kick-off, and it will no doubt prevent a lot of supporters from attending who otherwise would've.

The changing of schedules is completely out of any supporters' hands, and Vale have been shown absolutely no consideration or mercy in this instance.

'Joke' is extremely apt to describe this situation.

This change in kick-off time can be enough for anyone to fall out of love with football, especially if you're on the receiving end of it.

You'd do well to find a supporter, Vale or not, who would agree that this fixture change is a good idea, as once again, the supporters are not considered in this instance.

What do you make of the fixture change? Have Sky considered the Vale supporters in this? How would you feel if your team was on the receiving end? Share your thoughts below!

Whilst Vale supporters don't have to worry about the trip down to Devon for another few months, in the meantime, head coach Brady may well have helped his side turn a corner.

The Valiants won just one game in their opening nine fixtures this season, the sole victory incidentally coming against Exeter, but with five wins and one defeat in the following seven, Vale finally look to have found their feet.

By the time they travel to Exeter, supporters hope to be looking up instead of over their shoulder, as an immediate return to League One shouldn't yet be ruled out.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.
